# Awesome Awesome Isaac Sim [![Awesome](https://cdn.rawgit.com/sindresorhus/awesome/d7305f38d29fed78fa85652e3a63e154dd8e8829/media/badge.svg)](https://github.com/sindresorhus/awesome)[中文](./README_CN.md)
<a id="intro"></a>
## 📝 Introduction

NVIDIA Isaac Sim is a cutting-edge robotics simulation platform built on Omniverse, enabling high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated physics and sensor simulation for training and testing intelligent machines. With tight integration into the Isaac ecosystem—including Isaac Lab for reinforcement learning and Isaac Gym for scalable RL training—it empowers researchers and developers to prototype, validate, and deploy robotic systems faster and safer. This curated list highlights essential tools, tutorials, sample projects, and community contributions that unlock the full potential of Isaac Sim and its companion frameworks like NVIDIA Newton for advanced dynamics.

<a id="scope"></a>
## 🎯 Scope

- Official documentation, SDKs, and core components of Isaac Sim
- Tutorials, demos, and example environments for Isaac Sim, Isaac Lab, and Isaac Gym
- Open-source projects leveraging Isaac Sim for robotics research or applications
- Tools and extensions enhancing simulation fidelity (e.g., NVIDIA Newton, ROS/ROS 2 bridges)
- Community resources, workshops, and best practices for effective simulation workflows

<a id="criteria"></a>
## ✅ Inclusion Criteria

- Directly uses or significantly enhances Isaac Sim, Isaac Lab, Isaac Gym, or NVIDIA Newton
- Actively maintained or represents a notable reference implementation
- Provides clear educational value, reproducibility, or practical utility
- Open source or publicly accessible with permissive licensing
- Avoids generic robotics content not tied to the Isaac ecosystem

<a id="audience"></a>
## 👥 Audience

Robotics researchers, AI engineers, and developers building simulated or real-world autonomous systems using NVIDIA’s Isaac platform will find this list invaluable for accelerating development and staying current with best practices.

<a id="custom"></a>
## 📌 Additional Resources

- [Isaac Sim 官方文档](https://docs.isaacsim.omniverse.nvidia.com/) – Comprehensive documentation for NVIDIA Isaac Sim, covering installation, core features, and simulation workflows.
- [Isaac Lab 文档](https://isaac-sim.github.io/IsaacLab/) – Official documentation for Isaac Lab, a framework built on Isaac Sim for reinforcement learning and robotics research.
- [robotsfan](https://www.robotsfan.com) – A blog by Ziqi Fan featuring tutorials, project walkthroughs, and insights on using Isaac Sim and related NVIDIA robotics tools.

### Community & Support
- [NVIDIA Isaac Sim Discord (Official)](https://discord.gg/wtXUhcDh) – Active community for developers using Isaac Sim, hosted by NVIDIA.
- [Isaac Lab Discord](https://discord.gg/nyr6t33v) – Dedicated channel for Isaac Lab users to discuss RL, robotics, and simulation development.

### Tutorials & Learning Resources
- [NVIDIA Isaac Sim Tutorials (GitHub)](https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/IsaacSim-Samples) – Official sample scenes, scripts, and tutorials demonstrating sensor simulation, robot control, and USD workflows.
- [Isaac Gym: High-Performance RL Environments (Paper & Code)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.10470) – Research paper introducing Isaac Gym, with open-source environments for GPU-accelerated reinforcement learning; code available on [GitHub](https://github.com/NVIDIA-Omniverse/IsaacGymEnvs).
- [Isaac Lab GitHub Repository](https://github.com/isaac-sim/IsaacLab) – Source code and examples for Isaac Lab, including modular RL environments, asset management, and training pipelines.

### Key Papers & Technical References
- [NVIDIA Isaac Sim: A Scalable Simulation Platform for Robotics (White Paper)](https://developer.nvidia.com/isaac-sim-whitepaper) – Overview of Isaac Sim’s architecture, physics fidelity, and integration with Omniverse.
- [Newton: A Physics Engine for Large-Scale Simulation (Preview)](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/dir/newton/) – Introduction to NVIDIA Newton, the next-generation multi-physics engine designed for scalability and realism in robotic simulation (note: currently in preview/research phase).

### Additional Developer Resources
- [Omniverse Isaac Sim Forum](https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/c/isaac/isaac-sim/105) – Official NVIDIA developer forum for troubleshooting, feature requests, and community Q&A.
- [Isaac Sim YouTube Channel](https://www.youtube.com/playlist?list=PL_mF8d9aQWkKqyJzZ6eVfRmYwBxLqMjHg) – Video tutorials, demos, and conference talks from NVIDIA on Isaac Sim capabilities and use cases.

<a id="papers"></a>
## 🔥 Papers & Research

- ["BTGenBot-2: Efficient Behavior Tree Generation with Small Language Models" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01870v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.01870v1) — BTGenBot-2 introduces a lightweight, open-source 1B-parameter language model that generates executable behavior trees directly from natural language instructions and robot action primitives, enabling zero-shot task execution with error recovery. The work presents the first standardized benchmark for LLM-based behavior tree generation, evaluating performance across 52 navigation and manipulation tasks implemented in NVIDIA Isaac Sim. By achieving high success rates and significantly faster inference than larger models, BTGenBot-2 offers a practical solution for deploying language-driven robotic control on resource-constrained systems.

- ["PolicyFlow: Policy Optimization with Continuous Normalizing Flow in Reinforcement Learning" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01156v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.01156v1) — PolicyFlow introduces a novel on-policy reinforcement learning algorithm that integrates continuous normalizing flows (CNFs) with PPO-style objectives without requiring expensive likelihood evaluations along the full flow trajectory. The method approximates importance ratios using velocity field variations along an interpolation path and includes a Brownian Regularizer to promote behavioral diversity. Experiments include IsaacLab among other environments, demonstrating improved performance and stability. While IsaacLab is used as one of several evaluation platforms, the paper does not focus on Isaac Sim as a core contribution or system.

- ["WheelArm-Sim: A Manipulation and Navigation Combined Multimodal Synthetic Data Generation Simulator for Unified Control in Assistive Robotics" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21129v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.21129v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces WheelArm-Sim, a synthetic data generation framework built in Isaac Sim to support unified control of wheelchair-mounted robotic arms (WMRAs) for assistive robotics. It generates a multimodal dataset combining navigation and manipulation tasks—13 tasks, 232 trajectories, and over 67k samples—and demonstrates its utility with a baseline action prediction model for a mustard-picking task. By leveraging Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics and sensor simulation, the work enables scalable, realistic data collection for integrated assistive robot control.

- ["A Decentralized Multi-Robot Dataset for Early Deadlock Forecasting in Smart Factory Navigation" (2026)](https://openalex.org/W7126226733) — Zenodo (CERN European Organization for Nuclear Research) — This paper introduces a decentralized multi-robot dataset specifically designed for early deadlock forecasting in smart factory settings. The dataset is built using Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform, where two autonomous Carter robots navigate a shared environment under fixed high-level plans, generating rich multimodal data including trajectories, event labels, synchronized camera streams, and navigation context. By leveraging Isaac Sim alongside ROS 2 Humble and Nav2, the authors provide a realistic and reproducible benchmark for developing and evaluating early deadlock prediction models, neurosymbolic reasoning systems, and safety-aware navigation algorithms.

- ["Enhancing Control Policy Smoothness by Aligning Actions with Predictions from Preceding States" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18479v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.18479v1) — The paper introduces ASAP, a novel loss-based action smoothing method that reduces high-frequency oscillations in deep reinforcement learning policies by aligning actions with those from 'transition-induced similar states'—a data-driven notion of state similarity derived from actual environmental transitions. The method penalizes second-order action differences and is evaluated in both Gymnasium and Isaac Lab environments, showing improved smoothness and policy performance. Its explicit use of Isaac Lab as a core experimental platform makes it directly relevant to the Isaac Sim ecosystem.

- ["AION: Aerial Indoor Object-Goal Navigation Using Dual-Policy Reinforcement Learning" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15614v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.15614v1) — The paper proposes AION, a dual-policy reinforcement learning framework for vision-based aerial Object-Goal Navigation (ObjectNav) that operates without external localization or global maps. It decouples exploration and goal-reaching into specialized policies and is evaluated on AI2-THOR and high-fidelity drone simulations in Isaac Sim, demonstrating strong performance in navigation efficiency, exploration, and safety. The use of Isaac Sim for real-time validation with realistic drone dynamics highlights its role as a critical testing platform for aerial robot autonomy.

- ["Communication-Free Collective Navigation for a Swarm of UAVs via LiDAR-Based Deep Reinforcement Learning" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13657v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.13657v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces a communication-free deep reinforcement learning (DRL) controller for UAV swarm navigation in GPS-denied, obstacle-rich environments. The approach uses an implicit leader-follower model where only the leader knows the goal, and followers rely solely on onboard LiDAR and local perception. Trained in NVIDIA Isaac Sim, the DRL policy enables emergent flocking and obstacle avoidance behaviors without inter-agent communication or external localization, demonstrating strong sim-to-real transfer in real-world experiments with five UAVs.

- ["Transformable Quadruped Wheelchair: Unified Walking and Wheeled Locomotion via Mode-Conditioned Policy Distillation" (2026)](https://openalex.org/W7124139016) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1424-8220/26/2/566/pdf?version=1768397051) — Sensors — This paper presents a transformable quadruped wheelchair capable of switching between walking and wheeled locomotion modes to navigate diverse terrains. The authors use Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) in NVIDIA Isaac Sim to train separate policies for each mode, then distill them into a unified mode-conditioned policy. Simulation experiments in Isaac Sim validate improved mobility efficiency through adaptive mode switching, with vibration analysis confirming smoother wheeled travel and stable walking over uneven surfaces.

- ["Comprehensive Machine Learning Benchmarking for Fringe Projection Profilometry with Photorealistic Synthetic Data" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08900v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.08900v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces the first open-source, photorealistic synthetic dataset for fringe projection profilometry (FPP), explicitly generated using NVIDIA Isaac Sim to produce 15,600 fringe images and 300 depth reconstructions across 50 diverse objects. The authors benchmark four neural networks on single-shot depth reconstruction, revealing performance limitations of direct fringe-to-depth mapping and establishing standardized evaluation protocols. By leveraging Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated rendering and physics capabilities, the work provides a reproducible foundation for developing and comparing learning-based FPP methods.

- ["Robot-Assisted Suturing in Surgical Simulation: A Comparative Analysis between NVIDIA's Simulator and an Open Platform" (2026)](https://openalex.org/W7119741193) — Journal of Medical Robotics Research — This paper presents a comparative study of NVIDIA Isaac Sim and the open-source Asynchronous Multi-Body Framework (AMBF) for robot-assisted surgical suturing simulations. The authors recreate a detailed suturing environment in Isaac Sim—featuring dual robotic arms, an endoscope, surgical phantoms, and a thread-modeled needle—and enable haptic interaction via ROS. While Isaac Sim demonstrates superior visual fidelity, AMBF achieves higher simulation speeds (up to 15 FPS faster) and lower latency (~0.1 s) with haptic input. The work offers valuable insights into Isaac Sim’s trade-offs between realism and performance in surgical robotics applications.

- ["CoINS: Counterfactual Interactive Navigation via Skill-Aware VLM" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03956v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.03956v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper proposes CoINS, a hierarchical framework for interactive navigation that combines a skill-aware Vision-Language Model (InterNav-VLM) with a reinforcement learning-based skill library to enable robots to reason about and execute object interactions for path clearance. While the abstract mentions simulation-based training and evaluation, it does not explicitly state the use of Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, nor does it present Isaac Sim as a core component of the methodology or contributions.

- ["A Review of Online Diffusion Policy RL Algorithms for Scalable Robotic Control" (2026)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06133v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.06133v1) — This paper presents the first comprehensive review and empirical analysis of Online Diffusion Policy Reinforcement Learning (Online DPRL) algorithms for scalable robotic control. It introduces a novel taxonomy categorizing methods into four families and evaluates them through extensive experiments on a unified NVIDIA Isaac Lab benchmark across 12 diverse robotic tasks. The study assesses algorithms on task diversity, parallelization, diffusion step scalability, cross-embodiment generalization, and robustness, revealing key trade-offs and bottlenecks in current approaches.

- ["A Workshop on Planning and Operating Robot Manipulations Using AI and Isaac Sim" (2026)](https://openalex.org/W7126166415) — Learning and analytics in intelligent systems — This workshop explores the integration of AI techniques for planning and executing robot manipulation tasks, with a specific focus on leveraging NVIDIA's Isaac Sim as a core simulation platform. It brings together researchers and practitioners to discuss methodologies, tools, and case studies that utilize Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics and sensor simulation capabilities for developing and testing robotic manipulation algorithms. The event highlights Isaac Sim’s role in enabling scalable, realistic training and validation environments for AI-driven robotics.

- ["SCAFusion: A Multimodal 3D Detection Framework for Small Object Detection in Lunar Surface Exploration" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22503v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.22503v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — SCAFusion introduces a multimodal 3D object detection framework specifically designed to improve small object detection—such as meteor fragments and rocks—for lunar surface exploration. The method enhances BEVFusion with several novel components, including a Section-aware Coordinate Attention mechanism tailored for small, irregular targets. Crucially, the authors evaluate their approach in simulated lunar environments built using Isaac Sim, where SCAFusion achieves a 90.93% mAP, significantly outperforming the baseline. This demonstrates Isaac Sim’s utility as a high-fidelity testbed for off-world robotic perception systems.

- ["A Framework for Deploying Learning-based Quadruped Loco-Manipulation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18938v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.18938v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents an open-source framework for training, benchmarking, and deploying reinforcement learning (RL)-based whole-body controllers for quadruped loco-manipulation on the Unitree B1 robot with a Z1 arm. The authors re-implement a policy originally trained in Isaac Gym, extend it to MuJoCo via a hardware abstraction layer, and deploy it on real hardware using ROS. Their work highlights sim-to-sim discrepancies between Isaac Gym and MuJoCo contact models and demonstrates improved real-world manipulation through coordinated whole-body control, offering a reproducible pipeline for RL-based robotic research.

- ["Towards Senior-Robot Interaction: Reactive Robot Dog Gestures" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17136v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.17136v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a senior-oriented interaction system for quadruped robots that uses hand and head gestures for intuitive control and socially expressive robotic behaviors. The authors trained dog-like gestures—such as three-legged balancing and paw-lifting—using curriculum-based reinforcement learning in Isaac Gym, achieving over 95% success in simulation. They validated the paw-lift gesture on a real Unitree robot, demonstrating social expressiveness while identifying key sim-to-real challenges in balance and compliance. The work highlights Isaac Gym’s role in developing socially capable legged robots for elderly companionship.

- ["Automatic Reward Shaping from Multi-Objective Human Heuristics" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15120v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.15120v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces MORSE, a framework that automatically combines multiple human-designed heuristic rewards into a unified reward function via bi-level optimization, enhancing multi-objective reinforcement learning. It explicitly evaluates MORSE in Isaac Sim environments alongside MuJoCo, demonstrating competitive performance against manually tuned rewards across robotic tasks. The use of Isaac Sim as a core experimental platform for validating the method’s effectiveness in realistic, GPU-accelerated robotic simulations makes it directly relevant to the Awesome Isaac Sim list.

- ["Reinforcement Learning based 6-DoF Maneuvers for Microgravity Intravehicular Docking: A Simulation Study with Int-Ball2 in ISS-JEM" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13514v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.13514v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a reinforcement learning framework for 6-DoF docking of JAXA's Int-Ball2 robot inside a high-fidelity Isaac Sim simulation of the ISS Japanese Experiment Module (JEM). Using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO), the authors train policies under domain-randomized dynamics and observation noise while modeling propeller drag-torque effects, demonstrating reliable docking performance in microgravity conditions. The work leverages Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform to enable propulsion-accurate, physics-rich training for autonomous intravehicular navigation.

- ["Development of Real-World Implementation Technology for Isaac Sim-Based Autonomous Driving AI Using a Sim2Real Approach" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W7116850167) — Journal of The Korean Society of Manufacturing Technology Engineers — This paper presents a Sim2Real framework for deploying autonomous driving AI trained in NVIDIA Isaac Sim to real-world vehicles. The authors develop domain adaptation techniques and sensor modeling strategies within Isaac Sim to bridge the simulation-to-reality gap, validating their approach through real-world driving experiments. By leveraging Isaac Sim’s high-fidelity physics and sensor simulation capabilities, the work demonstrates a practical pipeline for transferring learned driving policies from simulation to physical autonomous systems.

- ["Entropy-Controlled Intrinsic Motivation Reinforcement Learning for Quadruped Robot Locomotion in Complex Terrains" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06486v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.06486v2) — The paper introduces Entropy-Controlled Intrinsic Motivation (ECIM), a reinforcement learning algorithm that enhances quadruped robot locomotion by integrating entropy-based adaptive exploration with intrinsic motivation to mitigate premature convergence. The method is evaluated in Isaac Gym across six challenging terrain types, demonstrating consistent improvements over PPO baselines—including higher task rewards, reduced body pitch oscillation, lower joint acceleration, and decreased torque consumption—leading to more stable and energy-efficient locomotion.

- ["An Integrated System for WEEE Sorting Employing X-ray Imaging, AI-based Object Detection and Segmentation, and Delta Robot Manipulation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05599v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.05599v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper presents an integrated system for automated WEEE sorting that combines dual-energy X-ray imaging, AI-based detection (YOLO and U-Net), and Delta robot manipulation to safely extract batteries from waste streams. While the authors validate their approach in a photorealistic simulation environment, the abstract does not explicitly mention Isaac Sim as the platform used—only stating 'a photorealistic simulation environment developed in' without completion. Without clear evidence of Isaac Sim being central to the methodology or experiments, the work remains outside the scope of an Awesome list focused specifically on Isaac Sim.

- ["Autonomous Planning In-space Assembly Reinforcement-learning free-flYer (APIARY) International Space Station Astrobee Testing" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.03729v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.03729v1) — This paper presents the APIARY experiment, which demonstrates the first on-orbit use of reinforcement learning (RL) to control a free-flying robot—NASA’s Astrobee—aboard the International Space Station. The RL policy was trained in NVIDIA Isaac Lab using Proximal Policy Optimization with domain randomization over goal poses and mass distributions to ensure robust 6-DOF control. The work validates Isaac Lab as a critical simulation platform for developing space-ready robotic autonomy, enabling rapid deployment of adaptive behaviors for in-space assembly and logistics.

- ["Autonomous Reinforcement Learning Robot Control with Intel's Loihi 2 Neuromorphic Hardware" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.03911v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.03911v1) — This paper presents a pipeline for converting reinforcement learning-trained Artificial Neural Networks (ANNs) into spiking Sigma-Delta Neural Networks (SDNNs) deployable on Intel's Loihi 2 neuromorphic hardware. The authors evaluate the converted policy in NVIDIA's Omniverse Isaac Lab simulation environment for closed-loop control of the Astrobee free-flying robot, comparing performance between GPU and Loihi 2 execution. The work demonstrates the feasibility of using neuromorphic hardware for energy-efficient robotic control, with Isaac Lab serving as the critical simulation platform for validation.

- ["RoboWheel: A Data Engine from Real-World Human Demonstrations for Cross-Embodiment Robotic Learning" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02729v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.02729v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — RoboWheel introduces a data engine that transforms human hand-object interaction videos into physically plausible, retargetable robotic trajectories for cross-embodiment learning. The method uses monocular RGB or RGB-D inputs to reconstruct interactions and refines them via reinforcement learning under contact constraints. Crucially, the pipeline leverages Isaac Sim to scale data diversity through simulation-augmented domain randomization across embodiments, objects, and environments, while preserving physical plausibility. This enables effective training of vision-language-action and imitation learning models with performance comparable to teleoperation data.

- ["CostNav: A Navigation Benchmark for Real-World Economic-Cost Evaluation of Physical AI Agents" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20216v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.20216v2) — CostNav introduces a novel navigation benchmark that evaluates physical AI agents through real-world economic cost-revenue analysis, integrating industry-standard financial and safety data with Isaac Sim's high-fidelity collision and cargo dynamics simulations. By leveraging Isaac Sim as the core simulation environment to model realistic physical interactions and operational costs, CostNav reveals significant gaps between traditional navigation success metrics and commercial viability—demonstrating negative contribution margins for standard Nav2 approaches. This work provides a method-agnostic platform to drive development of economically sustainable autonomous delivery systems.

- ["Digital twins as decision-support tools for automation in agriculture: A case study on robotic vaccination" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4416642291) — Smart Agricultural Technology — This paper presents a digital twin framework for a robotic vaccination system in agricultural feedlots, leveraging NVIDIA Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform. The authors integrate a robotic arm (Franka Emika Panda and a custom manipulator), a Detectron2-based deep learning model for neck segmentation, and reinforcement learning to enable precise vaccine targeting. By using Isaac Sim’s realistic, dynamic environments, they validate system accuracy and control, demonstrating a cost-effective pathway for deploying automation in livestock management.

- ["A Study on Bridging the Gap With Reinforcement Learning: TD3 Optimization for Sim-to-real Transfer from Gazebo to Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W7104761584) — Journal of Institute of Control Robotics and Systems — This paper investigates sim-to-real transfer by optimizing the TD3 reinforcement learning algorithm to bridge the gap between Gazebo and Isaac Sim environments. The authors train agents in both simulators and evaluate their performance on real-world robotic tasks, using Isaac Sim as a key high-fidelity simulation platform to improve policy robustness and transferability. The study highlights Isaac Sim's role in enabling more realistic dynamics and sensor modeling compared to Gazebo, ultimately enhancing real-world deployment success.

- ["Multi-Agent Deep Reinforcement Learning for Collision-Free Posture Control of Multi-Manipulators in Shared Workspaces" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415999376) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1424-8220/25/22/6822/pdf?version=1762524575) — Sensors — This paper proposes a multi-agent deep reinforcement learning (MADRL) framework for real-time, collision-free posture control of multiple manipulators in shared workspaces. It introduces a line-segment representation of manipulator links for efficient interlink distance computation and uses a centralized training with decentralized execution (CTDE) paradigm. The method is implemented and validated in NVIDIA Isaac Sim across various overlapping workspace scenarios, demonstrating faster learning convergence, improved computational efficiency, and reduced task completion time in pick-and-place tasks compared to conventional approaches.

- ["Isaac Lab: A GPU-Accelerated Simulation Framework for Multi-Modal Robot Learning" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04831v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.04831v1) — Isaac Lab is introduced as the successor to Isaac Gym, offering a GPU-accelerated, modular simulation framework tailored for large-scale multi-modal robot learning. It integrates high-fidelity physics, photorealistic rendering, sensor simulation, actuator models, and domain randomization within a unified platform for reinforcement and imitation learning. The framework supports diverse robotics challenges—from dexterous manipulation to whole-body control—and will integrate the differentiable Newton physics engine to enable gradient-based learning methods, significantly advancing scalable and data-efficient robotics research.

- ["Learning Spatial Awareness for Laparoscopic Surgery with AI Assisted Visual Feedback" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02233v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.02233v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces an AI-assisted training framework for laparoscopic surgery that enhances spatial awareness by integrating real-time 3D visual feedback with the standard 2D endoscopic view. Developed in NVIDIA Isaac Sim, the system uses AI modules to localize instruments and detect tissue interactions, triggering mixed-reality overlays when spatial misjudgments occur. The approach supports multiple surgical tasks and improves trainees’ depth perception and tool orientation without altering the clinical workflow.

- ["Reinforcement Learning for 12-DoF Ant Robot Locomotion in One-File Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W7117505902) — This paper presents a compact, single-file implementation of reinforcement learning for locomotion control of a 12-degree-of-freedom (DoF) Ant robot using NVIDIA's Isaac Sim. The work leverages Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics simulation to enable efficient training and evaluation of RL policies in a high-fidelity environment. By integrating the robot model, environment setup, and PPO-based learning algorithm into one accessible script, the project serves as an educational and practical template for rapid prototyping of legged locomotion in Isaac Sim.

- ["Real-DRL: Teach and Learn in Reality" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00112v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.00112v1) — This paper introduces Real-DRL, a framework for safe deep reinforcement learning in real-world autonomous systems that combines a DRL agent (DRL-Student), a physics-based safety policy (PHY-Teacher), and a Trigger mechanism to manage their interaction. The authors evaluate their approach on a physical quadruped robot, a cart-pole system, and a simulated quadruped in NVIDIA Isaac Gym, using the latter primarily as a benchmark environment rather than a core contribution or focus. While Isaac Gym is used for simulation experiments, the paper’s main innovation lies in its safety architecture, not in advancing or leveraging Isaac Sim’s capabilities.

- ["Towards Reinforcement Learning Based Log Loading Automation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26363v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26363v1) — This paper presents a reinforcement learning approach to automate the full log loading operation of forestry forwarders, extending beyond prior work on grasping alone. The authors developed a custom trailer-type forwarder simulation model and a realistic log-loading environment in NVIDIA's Isaac Gym to train agents using curriculum learning. Their best agent achieved a 94% success rate in autonomously locating, grappling, transporting, and depositing logs onto the forwarder bed, demonstrating Isaac Gym’s utility for complex, real-world robotic tasks in forestry automation.

- ["Towards An Adaptive Locomotion Strategy For Quadruped Rovers: Quantifying When To Slide Or Walk On Planetary Slopes" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18678v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.18678v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a preliminary study comparing the Cost of Transport (CoT) for walking versus torso-based sliding locomotion in quadruped rovers on planetary slopes with varying friction and speed conditions. The authors use physics-based simulations in Isaac Sim, complemented by particle interaction validation in ANSYS-Rocky, to identify threshold conditions where switching between locomotion strategies becomes energetically favorable. By leveraging Isaac Sim as a core simulation environment, the work contributes an initial framework for adaptive locomotion planning in challenging extraterrestrial terrains.

- ["GaussGym: An open-source real-to-sim framework for learning locomotion from pixels" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15352v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.15352v1) — GaussGym introduces a real-to-sim framework that integrates 3D Gaussian Splatting as a high-fidelity, high-speed renderer directly into IsaacGym, enabling photorealistic robot simulation at over 100,000 steps per second on consumer GPUs. The work leverages IsaacGym’s vectorized physics simulation to train locomotion policies from pixels, demonstrating improved navigation through rich visual semantics and seamless integration of real-world scans and generative scene data. By tightly coupling IsaacGym with advanced rendering, GaussGym bridges scalable simulation and realistic perception for sim-to-real robotics.

- ["DeGrip: A Compact Cable-driven Robotic Gripper for Desktop Disassembly" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16231v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.16231v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces DeGrip, a compact, cable-driven robotic gripper with three degrees of freedom specifically designed for disassembling end-of-life desktop computers. To evaluate its performance, the authors developed a dedicated disassembly environment in Isaac Sim, leveraging its GPU-accelerated physics simulation to test DeGrip’s ability to operate in confined spaces and handle components in arbitrary configurations. The results validate DeGrip’s effectiveness for real-world disassembly tasks, demonstrating the utility of Isaac Sim as a testing platform for specialized robotic hardware.

- ["UrbanVerse: Scaling Urban Simulation by Watching City-Tour Videos" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15018v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.15018v1) — UrbanVerse introduces a data-driven real-to-sim system that transforms crowd-sourced city-tour videos into high-fidelity, physics-aware urban simulation environments. Built on Isaac Sim, it includes UrbanVerse-100K—a repository of over 100k annotated 3D urban assets—and UrbanVerse-Gen, an automated pipeline that reconstructs metric-scale scenes from video. The system delivers 160 diverse, realistic urban scenes from 24 countries and demonstrates strong sim-to-real transfer, boosting navigation success by 30.1% in real-world trials with minimal intervention.

- ["EdgeNavMamba: Mamba Optimized Object Detection for Energy Efficient Edge Devices" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14946v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.14946v1) — EdgeNavMamba introduces a reinforcement learning framework for energy-efficient autonomous navigation on edge devices, featuring a compact Mamba-based object detector trained on a custom indoor shape dataset. The detector provides bounding boxes to an RL policy for goal-directed navigation and is evaluated in both real-world edge hardware (Jetson Orin Nano, Raspberry Pi 5) and simulators—including IsaacLab—where it achieves 31% fewer parameters than the baseline while maintaining high accuracy. The work demonstrates significant reductions in model size and energy consumption without sacrificing navigation performance.

- ["Adaptive Obstacle-Aware Task Assignment and Planning for Heterogeneous Robot Teaming" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14063v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.14063v1) — The paper introduces OATH, a novel framework for Multi-Agent Task Assignment and Planning (MATP) that improves scalability and adaptability in obstacle-rich environments through an adaptive Halton sequence map and a cluster-auction-selection mechanism. It integrates LLM-based human instruction interpretation with real-time planning and validates its approach extensively in NVIDIA Isaac Sim, demonstrating significant gains over existing MATP methods in task assignment quality and dynamic adaptability. The use of Isaac Sim as the primary simulation platform underscores its role in evaluating complex, heterogeneous robot teaming scenarios.

- ["Benchmarking Digital Twins for Tower Cranes: Isaac Sim vs. Gazebo" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415969566) — This paper presents a benchmark comparing Isaac Sim and Gazebo for developing digital twins of tower cranes, focusing on simulation fidelity, performance, and ease of integration with real-world sensor data. The authors implement identical crane models in both simulators and evaluate them across metrics like physics accuracy, rendering quality, and real-time capability. Their findings highlight Isaac Sim’s advantages in GPU-accelerated physics and photorealistic rendering, making it better suited for high-fidelity digital twin applications.

- ["Population-Coded Spiking Neural Networks for High-Dimensional Robotic Control" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10516v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.10516v1) — This paper introduces a novel framework combining population-coded Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) with Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) for energy-efficient robotic control, centered on the Population-coded Spiking Actor Network (PopSAN). The authors explicitly evaluate their method using the Isaac Gym platform—a core component of NVIDIA's Isaac Sim ecosystem—on the PixMC benchmark with high-dimensional manipulation tasks involving the Franka robotic arm. Their approach achieves up to 96.10% energy savings over traditional ANNs while maintaining competitive control performance, demonstrating robust trajectory tracking and stable manipulation in simulation.

- ["Integration of the TIAGo Robot into Isaac Sim with Mecanum Drive Modeling and Learned S-Curve Velocity Profiles" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10273v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.10273v2) — This paper presents the first integration of the PAL Robotics TIAGo++ Omni mobile manipulator into NVIDIA Isaac Sim, featuring a calibrated model that accurately replicates its mecanum-wheel omnidirectional drive dynamics. The authors develop two control models—a physically accurate wheel-based model and a lightweight velocity-based model—and use a learning-based approach to calibrate S-curve velocity profiles from minimal real-world trajectory data. By providing this Isaac Sim-ready model publicly, the work enables efficient simulation-based development and learning for TIAGo in diverse robotic tasks.

- ["PolySim: Bridging the Sim-to-Real Gap for Humanoid Control via Multi-Simulator Dynamics Randomization" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01708v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01708v3) — PolySim addresses the sim-to-real gap in humanoid whole-body control by training policies across multiple heterogeneous simulators simultaneously, reducing reliance on any single simulator's inductive bias. While Isaac Sim is used as one of the simulation engines in the multi-simulator setup, the paper does not focus on Isaac Sim as a core contribution or platform—it treats Isaac Sim as one component among others like MuJoCo. The method demonstrates improved zero-shot transfer to a real Unitree G1 robot, but Isaac Sim itself is not the central innovation or subject of study.

- ["Data-Efficient Multitask DAgger" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25466v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.25466v1) — This paper introduces a Data-Efficient Multitask DAgger framework that distills a single generalist policy from multiple task-specific experts by adaptively allocating demonstrations to tasks where the policy underperforms. The method employs a Kalman filter-based estimator to guide data collection and is validated on MetaWorld and a suite of drawer-opening tasks in Isaac Lab. Experiments show improved task success with fewer demonstrations, and the learned visual policy transfers zero-shot to a real robot with better performance than baseline methods.

- ["A Framework for Scalable Heterogeneous Multi-Agent Adversarial Reinforcement Learning in IsaacLab" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01264v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01264v1) — This paper introduces a framework for scalable heterogeneous multi-agent adversarial reinforcement learning within IsaacLab, extending it to support high-fidelity physics-based training of agents with asymmetric goals and capabilities. The authors develop adversarial MARL environments and integrate a competitive variant of HAPPO (Heterogeneous Agent PPO) to enable efficient policy training under adversarial dynamics. The work provides benchmarks, open-source code, and demonstrates robust policy learning in morphologically diverse competitive scenarios, enhancing IsaacLab’s utility for realistic multi-agent robotic simulation.

- ["DreamerNav: learning-based autonomous navigation in dynamic indoor environments using world models" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414525330) [PDF](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/robotics-and-ai/articles/10.3389/frobt.2025.1655171/pdf) (Citations: 1) — Frontiers in Robotics and AI — DreamerNav introduces a robot-agnostic navigation framework that extends DreamerV3 with multimodal perception and hybrid planning to enable robust autonomous navigation in dynamic indoor environments. The system is trained entirely in NVIDIA Isaac Sim using photorealistic, high-fidelity simulations with curriculum-based task complexity, allowing it to learn stochastic latent dynamics for long-horizon prediction and collision avoidance. Evaluated against strong baselines and validated on real quadrupedal robots without retraining, DreamerNav demonstrates high success rates and platform independence, highlighting Isaac Sim’s critical role in scalable, sim-to-real transfer.

- ["CGA-ASNet: an RGB-D amodal segmentation network for restoring occluded tomato regions" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414448697) [PDF](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/plant-science/articles/10.3389/fpls.2025.1664718/pdf) — Frontiers in Plant Science — The paper introduces CGA-ASNet, an RGB-D amodal segmentation network designed to reconstruct occluded tomato regions for accurate phenotyping. It leverages a synthetic dataset (Tomato-sim) generated using NVIDIA Isaac Sim’s Replicator Composer to simulate realistic tomato morphology and greenhouse conditions, enabling training without real-world annotations. The method incorporates a Contextual and Global Attention module to improve semantic understanding and localization, achieving strong performance on both synthetic and real-world data despite no explicit domain adaptation.

- ["DyDexHandover: Human-like Bimanual Dynamic Dexterous Handover using RGB-only Perception" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17350v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.17350v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — DyDexHandover introduces a multi-agent reinforcement learning framework for bimanual in-air object handover using only RGB perception, achieving human-like throwing and catching motions through human policy regularization. The method was trained and evaluated in a custom dual-arm simulation environment built in Isaac Sim, demonstrating 99% success on training objects and 75% on unseen objects. This work highlights Isaac Sim as the core platform for developing and testing complex dexterous manipulation policies with realistic physics and visual rendering.

- ["RL-augmented Adaptive Model Predictive Control for Bipedal Locomotion over Challenging Terrain" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18466v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.18466v1) — This paper proposes an RL-augmented Model Predictive Control (MPC) framework specifically designed for bipedal locomotion over challenging terrains such as stairs, stepping stones, and low-friction surfaces. The approach enhances a single-rigid-body-dynamics-based MPC by using reinforcement learning to adaptively parametrize system dynamics, swing leg control, and gait frequency. The method is validated in NVIDIA IsaacLab simulations, demonstrating superior robustness and adaptability compared to standalone MPC or RL baselines, offering a practical hybrid solution for complex bipedal navigation tasks.

- ["Robotic Skill Diversification via Active Mutation of Reward Functions in Reinforcement Learning During a Liquid Pouring Task" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18463v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.18463v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces a reward function mutation framework that applies Gaussian noise to reward term weights—accuracy, time, and effort—to generate diverse robotic pouring behaviors via reinforcement learning. The experiments are explicitly conducted in NVIDIA Isaac Sim using a Franka Emika Panda arm, with Proximal Policy Optimization as the learning algorithm. The resulting policies not only vary in pouring execution but also yield novel skills like rim cleaning and liquid mixing, demonstrating how reward-space exploration in Isaac Sim can foster skill diversification for adaptive robotic manipulation.

- ["Design and Implementation of Digital Twin System of OCS Maintenance Robot" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414345490) — Journal of Advanced Computational Intelligence and Intelligent Informatics — This paper presents a digital twin system for an overhead catenary system (OCS) maintenance robot designed to operate in the harsh plateau conditions of the Sichuan–Tibet Line. The authors use Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform to model the maintenance process, integrating virtual reality (VR) for error correction within the Isaac Sim environment. After virtual validation, real-world repairs are executed via rviz and physical robot collaboration, demonstrating Isaac Sim’s role in enabling safe, remote robotic maintenance in extreme environments.

- ["VIRTUS-FPP: Virtual Sensor Modeling for Fringe Projection Profilometry in NVIDIA Isaac Sim" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22685v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.22685v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — VIRTUS-FPP introduces the first physics-based virtual sensor modeling framework for Fringe Projection Profilometry (FPP) built entirely within NVIDIA Isaac Sim. The work leverages Isaac Sim’s physics-based rendering and programmable sensing to enable end-to-end simulation of FPP systems—from calibration to 3D reconstruction—with high fidelity to real-world optical behavior. By creating a digital twin of a physical FPP setup, the authors validate that virtual measurements closely match real ones, demonstrating Isaac Sim’s capability as a platform for rapid prototyping and development of structured light systems.

- ["Scalable Multi-Objective Robot Reinforcement Learning through Gradient Conflict Resolution" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14816v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.14816v1) — The paper introduces GCR-PPO, a novel actor-critic reinforcement learning method that resolves gradient conflicts among multiple objectives by decomposing updates using a multi-headed critic and prioritizing objectives. It explicitly evaluates the approach on IsaacLab’s manipulation and locomotion benchmarks, demonstrating improved scalability and performance over standard PPO, especially in tasks with high objective conflict. The work directly leverages Isaac Sim’s IsaacLab framework as a core experimental platform, validating its contributions on established GPU-accelerated robotic simulation environments.

- ["Dynamic Scene 3D Reconstruction of an Uncooperative Resident Space Object" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.07932v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.07932v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper evaluates state-of-the-art 3D reconstruction algorithms for dynamic scenes involving tumbling uncooperative Resident Space Objects (RSOs), which are critical for On-Orbit Servicing and Active Debris Removal missions. The authors developed a custom simulation environment in Isaac Sim to generate physics-accurate, realistic 2D image sequences of rotating satellites under orbital lighting conditions, using these datasets to test reconstruction fidelity. Preliminary results with Neuralangelo on static scenes show high-quality 3D meshes that closely match ground-truth CAD models, establishing a baseline for ongoing dynamic reconstruction studies.

- ["Performance Characterization of the LEAP Hand: Control Interface and Digital Twin Integration" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415398891) — This paper presents a comprehensive performance characterization of the LEAP Hand, introducing a user-friendly control interface with user-defined motion capabilities and integrating it with NVIDIA's Isaac Sim to enable digital twin-based development. The integration with Isaac Sim facilitates GPU-accelerated simulation for testing complex movements before hardware deployment. Long-duration experiments validate the system’s precision, dynamic tracking, and thermal behavior, providing practical insights for optimizing dexterous robotic hand control strategies.

- ["Real-Time Buoyancy Estimation for AUV Simulations Using Convex Hull-Based Submerged Volume Calculation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03804v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.03804v1) — This paper introduces a real-time buoyancy estimation method for AUV simulations by dynamically computing submerged volume using a convex hull-based approach. The technique extracts mesh geometry from NVIDIA Isaac Sim and calculates the intersection with the water surface along the z-axis, enabling accurate buoyant force updates that respond to vehicle orientation, depth, and wave motion. By avoiding precomputed hydrodynamic models and reducing computational load via cross-sectional area approximation, it enhances underwater simulation fidelity in Isaac Sim, addressing a key gap in its native physics capabilities.

- ["Learning to Coordinate: Distributed Meta-Trajectory Optimization Via Differentiable ADMM-DDP" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.01630v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.01630v2) — The paper proposes Learning to Coordinate (L2C), a meta-learning framework that automates hyperparameter tuning in distributed trajectory optimization for multi-agent systems by differentiating through the ADMM-DDP pipeline. It demonstrates L2C’s effectiveness on a cooperative aerial transport task using high-fidelity simulations in Isaac Sim, where it enables dynamic quadrotor formation reconfiguration for 6-DoF load manipulation in confined spaces and adapts to varying team sizes and conditions. The method achieves up to 88% faster gradient computation than existing approaches while ensuring dynamically feasible trajectories.

- ["Pallet Detection and Pose Estimation System Based on Synthetic Data from Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415883711) — This paper presents a pallet detection and pose estimation system that leverages synthetic data generated using NVIDIA Isaac Sim. The authors create a realistic virtual environment in Isaac Sim to produce labeled RGB-D images of pallets under various conditions, which are then used to train a deep learning model for real-world deployment. By utilizing Isaac Sim's high-fidelity rendering and physics capabilities, the system achieves robust performance without requiring extensive real-world annotated data, demonstrating the platform's value for scalable synthetic dataset generation in industrial robotics applications.

- ["Language-Enhanced Mobile Manipulation for Efficient Object Search in Indoor Environments" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20899v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.20899v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces GODHS, a language-enhanced hierarchical navigation framework that uses large language models to guide object search in unfamiliar indoor environments by integrating semantic perception and spatial reasoning. The authors evaluate their approach in Isaac Sim, demonstrating improved search efficiency over non-semantic baselines through a heuristic motion planner tailored for mobile manipulation. This work highlights Isaac Sim as a key platform for validating complex, language-driven robotic behaviors in photorealistic, physics-enabled indoor scenes.

- ["The Metaverse in Smart Industrial Environments: from Vision to Proof-of-Concept" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415034459) — This paper proposes an architecture for the Industrial Metaverse within the Cloud Continuum and demonstrates a proof-of-concept implementation that integrates NVIDIA Isaac Sim—built on Omniverse—with real ROS-based robotic systems. The work showcases how Isaac Sim enables immersive simulation and Digital Twin interaction in smart industrial environments, providing performance metrics and practical insights into deploying metaverse technologies for industrial operations, training, and decision-making.

- ["HumanoidVerse: A Versatile Humanoid for Vision-Language Guided Multi-Object Rearrangement" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16943v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.16943v1) — HumanoidVerse introduces a vision-language guided framework for long-horizon, multi-object rearrangement using a single humanoid robot in diverse simulated environments. The method leverages a dual-teacher distillation pipeline and a multi-stage curriculum to enable seamless sub-task transitions without environment resets, trained on a large-scale dataset of 350 tasks across four room layouts. Evaluated extensively in Isaac Gym—a core component of the Isaac Sim ecosystem—the approach significantly outperforms prior work in task success and spatial precision, demonstrating strong generalization to unseen instructions and scenes.

- ["Mind and Motion Aligned: A Joint Evaluation IsaacSim Benchmark for Task Planning and Low-Level Policies in Mobile Manipulation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15663v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.15663v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces Kitchen-R, a novel benchmark for evaluating integrated task planning and low-level control in mobile manipulation using complex language instructions. Built as a digital twin in Isaac Sim, it features over 500 instructions and supports a mobile manipulator robot, enabling three evaluation modes: planning-only, control-only, and full-system integration. The work provides baseline methods using a vision-language model for planning and a diffusion policy for control, along with a trajectory collection system, offering a realistic and holistic platform for embodied AI research.

- ["Isaac Sim-to-Real: Reinforcement Learning based Locomotion for Quadrupeds" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414432529) — This paper presents a reinforcement learning approach for quadrupedal locomotion that leverages NVIDIA Isaac Sim as the primary simulation environment for training and sim-to-real transfer. The authors develop policies in Isaac Sim using domain randomization and system identification techniques to bridge the reality gap, then deploy them on a physical quadruped robot with minimal fine-tuning. The work demonstrates Isaac Sim's effectiveness as a high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated platform for developing real-world robotic locomotion controllers.

- ["Simulation-To-Reality Hyperparameter Optimization of MPPI Controllers via Bayesian Optimization in NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414432302) — This paper presents a simulation-to-reality transfer approach for Model Predictive Path Integral (MPPI) controllers by optimizing hyperparameters using Bayesian Optimization within NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim. The authors leverage Isaac Sim's high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated physics to efficiently tune controller parameters in simulation before deploying them on real robotic systems, significantly reducing real-world trial-and-error. The work demonstrates improved task performance and sample efficiency, highlighting Isaac Sim as a critical platform for bridging the sim-to-real gap in robotic control.

- ["Sem-Geo-Nav: A Semantically and Geometrically Aware Navigation Framework for Legged Robots" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415598523) — The paper introduces Sem-Geo-Nav, a navigation framework that integrates semantic and geometric information—including occupancy, semantic class, elevation, and obstacle clearance maps—to generate a traversability map tailored for legged robots. The authors validate their approach in Omniverse Isaac Sim, demonstrating the robot’s ability to navigate complex indoor environments by pushing through, climbing over, or moving under obstacles, achieving up to 99.15% environment coverage. This work highlights Isaac Sim as a critical testbed for evaluating advanced navigation strategies in realistic, physics-rich simulated environments.

- ["Fully Spiking Actor-Critic Neural Network for Robotic Manipulation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12038v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.12038v1) — This paper proposes a fully spiking actor-critic neural network within a hybrid curriculum reinforcement learning framework for robotic manipulation tasks using a 9-DoF arm. The method leverages Isaac Gym—a component of the Isaac Sim ecosystem—for simulation-based training and evaluation, demonstrating improved energy efficiency, reduced latency, and competitive performance against ANN-based PPO baselines in target reaching and grasping tasks. The work highlights Isaac Gym’s utility for scalable, physics-accurate RL experiments with biologically inspired neural architectures.

- ["GBC: Generalized Behavior-Cloning Framework for Whole-Body Humanoid Imitation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09960v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.09960v1) — The paper introduces the Generalized Behavior Cloning (GBC) framework, a unified solution for whole-body humanoid imitation that bridges human motion capture data to robot control across diverse morphologies. It features an adaptive retargeting pipeline using differentiable inverse kinematics, a novel DAgger-MMPPO algorithm with MMTransformer architecture, and—critically—is implemented as an open-source platform built on Isaac Lab. This integration enables end-to-end training and deployment via configuration scripts, demonstrating high-fidelity policy transfer across multiple humanoid robots and establishing a practical foundation for generalized humanoid control.

- ["Fusion Robotics: Analysing Mobility Modes of an End-Over-End Walking Manipulator for Maintenance and Decommissioning" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4413325946) — Lecture notes in computer science — This paper investigates the mobility modes of an end-over-end walking manipulator designed for maintenance and decommissioning tasks in challenging environments. While the study focuses on robotic locomotion and manipulation strategies, it does not explicitly mention or utilize Isaac Sim as a simulation platform, experimental environment, or core methodology. The work appears to rely on alternative simulation or analytical approaches, with no indication that Isaac Sim contributed to the development, testing, or validation of the system.

- ["CleanUpBench: Embodied Sweeping and Grasping Benchmark" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05543v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.05543v1) — CleanUpBench introduces a novel embodied AI benchmark focused on realistic indoor cleaning tasks using a dual-mode mobile robot capable of sweeping and grasping. Built explicitly on NVIDIA Isaac Sim, it provides manually designed and procedurally generated environments to evaluate agents on task completion, spatial efficiency, motion quality, and control performance. The benchmark includes baseline agents and aims to bridge the gap between academic research and real-world deployment of service robots.

- ["UniFucGrasp: Human-Hand-Inspired Unified Functional Grasp Annotation Strategy and Dataset for Diverse Dexterous Hands" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03339v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.03339v2) — UniFucGrasp introduces a human-hand-inspired annotation strategy and dataset for functional grasping across diverse dexterous robotic hands, emphasizing task-relevant grasps like opening bottles rather than just stability. The method uses biomimetic motion mapping and geometry-based force closure to generate functional, stable grasps and is validated through experiments in Isaac Sim, demonstrating improved manipulation accuracy, cross-hand adaptability, and reduced annotation costs. This work provides the first multi-hand functional grasp dataset and shows practical value for training versatile robotic manipulation policies in simulation.

- ["Learning stable bipedal locomotion skills for quadrupedal robots on challenging terrains with automatic fall recovery" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4412840387) [PDF](https://www.nature.com/articles/s44182-025-00043-2.pdf) (Citations: 3) — npj Robotics — This paper presents a reinforcement learning approach to enable quadrupedal robots to perform stable bipedal locomotion on challenging terrains while incorporating automatic fall recovery. The authors train and validate their policies using NVIDIA Isaac Sim, leveraging its high-fidelity physics and GPU-accelerated simulation capabilities to handle complex terrain interactions and dynamic balance control. The integration with Isaac Sim allows for efficient policy transfer to real-world robots and robust handling of contact-rich scenarios.

- ["Benchmarking Massively Parallelized Multi-Task Reinforcement Learning for Robotics Tasks" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23172v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.23172v2) — The paper introduces MTBench, a large-scale benchmark for massively parallelized multi-task reinforcement learning in robotics, featuring 70 diverse tasks (50 manipulation and 20 locomotion) implemented specifically in Isaac Gym. It integrates four base RL algorithms with seven state-of-the-art MTRL methods to enable systematic evaluation of on-policy approaches under high-throughput GPU-accelerated simulation. By leveraging Isaac Sim's underlying physics engine through Isaac Gym, the benchmark demonstrates accelerated training and evaluation while revealing new challenges in multi-task policy generalization.

- ["Digital Twin Robotics: Immersive Software-in-the-Loop Testing with OpenUSD, Isaac Sim, and ROS" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4412900330) [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3721251.3734066?download=true) — This paper presents a digital twin framework for robotics that integrates OpenUSD, NVIDIA Isaac Sim, and ROS to enable immersive software-in-the-loop (SiL) testing. The authors leverage Isaac Sim as the core simulation environment to create high-fidelity, real-time virtual replicas of robotic systems, facilitating seamless interaction with ROS-based control stacks. By combining OpenUSD’s interoperable 3D scene description with Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics and rendering, the approach supports scalable, photorealistic validation of robotic behaviors before physical deployment.

- ["Shared Control of Holonomic Wheelchairs through Reinforcement Learning" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17055v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.17055v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper proposes a reinforcement learning-based shared control method for holonomic wheelchairs that translates 2D user input into 3D motion while prioritizing comfort and reducing cognitive load. The approach is trained in Isaac Gym—a key component of the NVIDIA Isaac Sim ecosystem—and evaluated in Gazebo simulation, with successful sim-to-real transfer demonstrated on a physical omnidirectional wheelchair. The work highlights Isaac Gym’s utility for training RL agents in complex, physics-rich environments relevant to assistive robotics.

- ["Digital Twin-Enabled Adaptive Robotics: Leveraging Large Language Models in Isaac Sim for Unstructured Environments" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4415775569) (Citations: 2) — Machines — This paper presents a digital twin framework that integrates real-time perception, a locally hosted large language model (Mistral 7B), and Isaac Sim to enable adaptive robotic operation in unstructured environments, specifically demonstrated in a hospital lab for microscope slide sorting. Isaac Sim is used for high-fidelity virtual environment synchronization, achieving 98.11% joint movement alignment between the physical YuMi robot and its digital counterpart. The system supports bi-directional physical-digital synchronization and intuitive voice-based human–robot interaction, showcasing Isaac Sim’s role in enabling responsive, LLM-augmented robotics applications.

- ["Probabilistic Human Intent Prediction for Mobile Manipulation: An Evaluation with Human-Inspired Constraints" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10131v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.10131v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces GUIDER, a probabilistic framework for estimating human intent in mobile manipulation tasks by maintaining dual belief layers for navigation and manipulation goals. The authors evaluate GUIDER in Isaac Sim across 25 human-robot interaction trials, demonstrating significant improvements over baselines in intent stability during both navigation and manipulation phases, particularly in redirection and geometry-constrained scenarios. Their use of Isaac Sim as the primary evaluation platform highlights its role in testing human-inspired robotic systems under realistic simulated conditions.

- ["Automated Behaviour-Driven Acceptance Testing of Robotic Systems" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05125v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.05125v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper proposes a behavior-driven development (BDD) framework for automated acceptance testing of robotic systems, using domain-specific modeling and knowledge graphs to represent composable test criteria. The authors integrate Isaac Sim as a core component of their software architecture to execute generated acceptance tests in simulation, specifically evaluating pick-and-place tasks under varying agent and environmental conditions. By combining a custom domain-specific language with Isaac Sim’s high-fidelity GPU-accelerated simulation, the approach enables systematic, automated validation of robotic behaviors, enhancing reliability and reducing manual testing effort.

- ["A multimodal digital twin for autonomous micro-drilling in scientific exploration" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4411701295) [PDF](https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s11548-025-03465-3.pdf) — International Journal of Computer Assisted Radiology and Surgery — This paper presents a multimodal digital twin for autonomous micro-drilling that combines the AMBF simulator for haptic and volumetric drilling with Isaac Sim for photorealistic rendering. The system generates synthetic visual and audio data to train AI models, evaluated using an eggshell drilling task as a proxy for cranial window creation in mice. While Isaac Sim is used for rendering, it serves as a supporting component rather than the core focus of the work.

- ["Bridging the Sim-to-Real Gap for Athletic Loco-Manipulation" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414050931) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2025.xxi.125) (Citations: 2) — This paper investigates methods to bridge the sim-to-real gap specifically for athletic loco-manipulation tasks, which combine dynamic locomotion and object interaction. While the abstract does not explicitly mention Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform or make a contribution centered on it, the work likely leverages advanced simulation environments. However, without clear evidence that Isaac Sim is integral to the methodology, dataset, or experimental validation, its relevance to an Awesome list focused on Isaac Sim is insufficient.

- ["ViTaSCOPE: Visuo-tactile Implicit Representation for In-hand Pose and Extrinsic Contact Estimation" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414050430) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2025.xxi.054) (Citations: 3) — ViTaSCOPE introduces a visuo-tactile implicit representation to jointly estimate in-hand object pose and extrinsic contact points during manipulation. The method fuses visual and tactile data using neural implicit fields, enabling accurate 6D pose tracking and contact localization even under occlusion. While the paper leverages simulation for training and validation, it does not explicitly use or focus on Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, instead relying on general-purpose simulators or custom setups.

- ["GRADE: Generating Realistic and Dynamic Environments for robotics research with Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4411616910) (Citations: 1) — The International Journal of Robotics Research — The paper introduces GRADE, a highly customizable framework built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim for generating realistic and dynamic environments tailored to robotics research. It leverages Isaac Sim’s rendering capabilities, physics engine, and low-level APIs to support synthetic data generation, active SLAM evaluation, and multi-robot scenarios, while introducing a novel method for varying environmental conditions across repeated experiments within physically accurate simulations. This enables continuous testing, development, and creation of high-fidelity, annotated datasets for perception-driven robotics.

- ["Robotic Simulation Systems and Intelligent Offline Teaching for Urban Rail Transit Maintenance" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4411352922) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2079-9292/14/12/2431/pdf?version=1749896318) — Electronics — This paper presents a robotic simulation system for urban rail transit maintenance that combines Gazebo and Isaac Sim to enable efficient offline teaching of undercarriage inspection robots. While Gazebo supports lightweight algorithm development, Isaac Sim provides high-fidelity rendering and robust physics for complex large-scale metro scenarios. The work includes environment data processing, point cloud mesh repair, robot modeling, sensor configuration, and a target vector labeling platform, culminating in an offline teaching system that reduces programming time from 5 minutes to 30 seconds per manipulator motion.

- ["SGN-CIRL: Scene Graph-based Navigation with Curriculum, Imitation, and Reinforcement Learning" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04505v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.04505v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — SGN-CIRL introduces a novel navigation framework that integrates 3D scene graphs with curriculum, imitation, and reinforcement learning to enable mapless robot navigation in partially observable environments. The method leverages open-vocabulary 3D scene graph representations to improve target localization and path planning, with training accelerated through expert demonstrations and progressively complex tasks. Evaluated in Isaac Sim, the approach demonstrates significantly higher success rates in challenging navigation scenarios compared to baselines, showcasing Isaac Sim’s utility for realistic, physics-based robotic learning experiments.

- ["Bridging Virtual and Physical Robotics: An AI-Driven Educational Platform Using NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim and Jetson Orin Nano" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4413918282) — This paper presents an AI-driven educational platform that integrates NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim with Jetson Orin Nano to bridge virtual and physical robotics learning. The authors leverage Isaac Sim as the core simulation environment for developing and testing robot behaviors, which are then deployed on real hardware via the Jetson platform. The work emphasizes accessible, hands-on robotics education by combining high-fidelity GPU-accelerated simulation with edge AI deployment, offering a practical pipeline for students and educators.

- ["Model-Based System Engineering Framework for Verification and Validation of Cyber-Physical Vehicle Systems" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414494115) — ASME Letters in Translational Robotics — This paper presents a digital twin-based model-based systems engineering framework for the verification and validation of autonomous vehicle systems, with a focus on modular design and requirements traceability. The authors apply their approach to an off-road path-planning problem, using NVIDIA Isaac Sim alongside MATLAB/Simulink and AutoDrive Simulator as key simulation environments to prototype and evaluate path-planning algorithms across diverse terrain conditions. While Isaac Sim is used as one of several tools, it is not the central focus or contribution of the work.

- ["FastTD3: Simple, Fast, and Capable Reinforcement Learning for Humanoid Control" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22642v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.22642v3) — FastTD3 introduces a streamlined and efficient off-policy reinforcement learning algorithm that accelerates humanoid control training by leveraging parallel simulation, large-batch updates, a distributional critic, and tuned hyperparameters. The method is explicitly evaluated in IsaacLab, among other simulation suites, demonstrating sub-3-hour convergence on HumanoidBench tasks using a single A100 GPU. Its simplicity, speed, and open implementation make it a practical tool for robotics RL research involving Isaac Sim environments.

- ["IndustryEQA: Pushing the Frontiers of Embodied Question Answering in Industrial Scenarios" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20640v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.20640v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — IndustryEQA introduces the first Embodied Question Answering benchmark tailored for safety-critical industrial warehouse environments, addressing a gap in existing household-focused EQA datasets. Built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim, it provides high-fidelity episodic videos with dynamic human agents, industrial assets, and hazardous scenarios grounded in real-world safety protocols, along with 1,344 annotated question-answer pairs across six reasoning categories. The benchmark includes baseline models and a comprehensive evaluation framework to advance the development of safety-aware, industrially applicable embodied agents.

- ["Omni-Perception: Omnidirectional Collision Avoidance for Legged Locomotion in Dynamic Environments" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19214v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.19214v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces Omni-Perception, an end-to-end locomotion policy that enables omnidirectional collision avoidance for legged robots by directly processing raw LiDAR point clouds using a novel perception module called PD-RiskNet. The authors develop a high-fidelity LiDAR simulation toolkit with realistic noise and fast raycasting, explicitly noting compatibility with Isaac Gym among other platforms to support scalable training and sim-to-real transfer. While Isaac Gym is mentioned as a compatible environment, the work does not focus on Isaac Sim as a core contribution or experimental platform.

- ["GenPO: Generative Diffusion Models Meet On-Policy Reinforcement Learning" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18763v4) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.18763v4) — GenPO introduces a generative policy optimization framework that integrates diffusion models into on-policy reinforcement learning by enabling exact log-likelihood computation through invertible action mappings. The method leverages IsaacLab as the primary simulation platform, conducting extensive experiments across eight robotic benchmarks including legged locomotion and dexterous manipulation tasks. By addressing key challenges in entropy estimation and KL regularization, GenPO demonstrates how diffusion policies can be effectively trained in large-scale, GPU-accelerated on-policy settings like those provided by IsaacLab.

- ["H2-COMPACT: Human-Humanoid Co-Manipulation via Adaptive Contact Trajectory Policies" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17627v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.17627v1) — H2-COMPACT introduces a hierarchical policy-learning framework enabling a legged humanoid to co-manipulate extended loads with a human using only haptic feedback. The lower-tier policy is trained in Isaac Gym—a GPU-accelerated reinforcement learning environment within the Isaac Sim ecosystem—under randomized payloads and friction conditions, then validated in MuJoCo and on a real Unitree G1 robot. By decoupling intent inference from locomotion control, the method achieves human-level cooperative carrying performance without motion capture, demonstrating Isaac Gym’s utility for training robust, full-body humanoid policies.

- ["RoboRAN: A Unified Robotics Framework for Reinforcement Learning-Based Autonomous Navigation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14526v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.14526v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — RoboRAN introduces a unified framework for training and deploying reinforcement learning-based navigation policies across diverse robotic platforms and environments, including terrestrial, aquatic, and space domains. The work explicitly leverages Isaac Lab—a component of the Isaac Sim ecosystem—as the simulation backbone for policy training and releases the first open-source API to deploy Isaac Lab-trained policies to real robots, enabling sim-to-real transfer. By providing standardized tasks, metrics, and a modular architecture, RoboRAN enhances reproducibility and cross-platform comparison, significantly lowering barriers to real-world RL deployment in robotics.

- ["Composing Dextrous Grasping and In-Hand Manipulation via Scoring with a Reinforcement Learning Critic" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4413945820) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.13253) (Citations: 1) — This paper proposes a method to bridge grasping and in-hand manipulation by using the critic network of a reinforcement learning agent—trained for in-hand manipulation—to score and select effective initial grasps. The approach significantly boosts manipulation success rates without extra training and demonstrates a real-world autonomous pipeline for grasping and reorienting challenging objects. However, the work does not mention or utilize Isaac Sim as a core component in its methodology, experiments, or implementation.

- ["Depth Transfer: Learning to See Like a Simulator for Real-World Drone Navigation" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12428v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.12428v1) — The paper introduces a depth transfer method using domain adaptation to align simulated and real-world depth data for drone navigation, leveraging a VAE to encode simulation depth into a latent space used by an RL policy. The approach is trained and evaluated in IsaacGym, where it significantly improves obstacle avoidance when switching from ground-truth to stereo depth, and successfully transfers to other simulators and real-world environments without policy fine-tuning. This work demonstrates IsaacGym as a critical platform for developing and validating sim-to-real vision-based policies.

- ["Learning to Walk with Hybrid Serial-Parallel Linkages: a Case Study on the Kangaroo Robot" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4414969711) — HAL (Le Centre pour la Communication Scientifique Directe) — This paper presents an end-to-end reinforcement learning pipeline for training walking policies on Kangaroo, a 72-DoF bipedal robot with hybrid serial-parallel leg linkages, using the Isaac Lab framework and leveraging Isaac Sim's built-in constraint modeling capabilities. The authors conduct ablation studies on observation states and validate trained policies in MuJoCo to assess sim-to-sim transfer, highlighting the importance of tuning simulation parameters like constraint stiffness and action rate penalties. The work demonstrates robust policy transfer across simulators despite differences in contact and constraint handling, offering insights into RL for complex robotic morphologies.

- ["PROBE: Proprioceptive Obstacle Detection and Estimation while Navigating in Clutter" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11848v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.11848v1) — The paper introduces PROBE, a Transformer-based method that enables robots to detect and estimate occluded rectangular obstacles using only proprioceptive data—specifically, historical torque and whole-body motion signals—without relying on vision.
