The approach is evaluated in simulated environments using Isaac Gym and validated on a real Unitree Go1 quadruped robot. By leveraging Isaac Gym for training and testing, the work demonstrates how high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated simulation can support proprioception-only perception tasks in cluttered scenarios.

- ["Surgical Robotics Environment in NVIDIA Isaac Sim for Robot-Assisted Suturing" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4411272480) — This paper presents a specialized surgical robotics environment built within NVIDIA Isaac Sim, designed specifically for robot-assisted suturing tasks. The authors leverage Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics and rendering capabilities to create a high-fidelity simulation framework that supports realistic tissue deformation, instrument interaction, and real-time visual feedback. The environment enables training and evaluation of autonomous or teleoperated suturing policies in a safe, reproducible setting, offering a valuable platform for advancing surgical robotics research.

- ["Web2Grasp: Learning Functional Grasps from Web Images of Hand-Object Interactions" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05517v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.05517v2) — Web2Grasp introduces a method to learn functional grasps for multi-finger robot hands by leveraging 3D reconstructions of human hand-object interactions from web images, avoiding the need for expensive teleoperated demonstrations. The approach retargets human grasps to robot hands and refines object meshes, then uses the initial model to generate additional physically plausible grasps in Isaac Gym—NVIDIA's high-throughput GPU-accelerated simulator—to expand the dataset for both seen and unseen objects. This pipeline enables effective training on diverse, underrepresented objects and demonstrates significant improvements in grasp success and functionality in simulation.

- ["MULE: Multi-terrain and Unknown Load Adaptation for Effective Quadrupedal Locomotion" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00488v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00488v1) — The paper proposes MULE, an adaptive reinforcement learning framework that enables quadrupedal robots to handle varying payloads and diverse terrains without predefined gait schedules. It uses a two-policy architecture—nominal and adaptive—to maintain stability and improve command tracking under dynamic conditions. The approach is validated through large-scale simulations in Isaac Gym and real-world tests on a Unitree Go1 robot across flat ground, slopes, and stairs, demonstrating superior robustness and adaptability compared to baseline controllers.

- ["Compound Koopman data-driven control for an inchworm robot: validation through virtual experiments" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4410506136) — Robotica — This paper proposes a novel data-driven compound control system combining fractional PID (FPID) control with Koopman operator theory to address the nonlinear dynamics of inchworm robots. The authors use a deep neural network to approximate the Koopman operator, enabling linear control design in a transformed space, and validate their approach through virtual experiments in NVIDIA Isaac Sim. Results show improved tracking accuracy and locomotion efficiency over existing methods, demonstrating Isaac Sim’s utility for validating advanced robotic control strategies in complex, simulated environments.

- ["Efficient trajectory planning for a 4-DOF robotic arm with curve interpolation and Gaussian process inference for pick-and-place manipulation tasks" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4410412228) — Robotica — This paper proposes an efficient trajectory planning method for a 4-DOF robotic arm using Bézier and Hermite interpolation combined with Gaussian process inference to generate smooth, feasible paths for pick-and-place tasks. The approach is evaluated in NVIDIA Isaac Sim, demonstrating its effectiveness in producing low-deviation, smooth trajectories where traditional optimization or data-driven methods face limitations. The use of Isaac Sim as the simulation platform validates the method’s practical applicability in robotic manipulation scenarios.

- ["High-Performance Reinforcement Learning on Spot: Optimizing Simulation Parameters with Distributional Measures" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17857v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.17857v3) — This paper presents a high-performance reinforcement learning approach for Boston Dynamics' Spot robot, using distributional measures like Wasserstein Distance and Maximum Mean Discrepancy to minimize the sim2real gap. The authors optimize simulation parameters in NVIDIA Isaac Lab via Covariance Matrix Adaptation Evolution Strategy, enabling training of agile locomotion policies that achieve over 5.2 m/s—more than triple Spot’s default speed—and demonstrate robustness to disturbances and slippery terrain. Training code is released through Isaac Lab, making it a core platform for their methodology.

- ["Performance Analysis of a Mass-Spring-Damper Deformable Linear Object Model in Robotic Simulation Frameworks" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13659v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.13659v1) — This paper presents a preliminary performance analysis of a Mass-Spring-Damper (MSD) model for simulating Deformable Linear Objects (DLOs) like cables and wires in robotic tasks. It explicitly uses Isaac Sim alongside Gazebo to collect force data through domain randomization, evaluating how parameter variations affect DLO dynamics and the transferability of simulation-trained policies. The work highlights Isaac Sim's role in enabling efficient, physics-based DLO simulation for robot learning.

- ["Reducing the Sim2Real gap for vacuum grasping in Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4410298109) — This paper introduces a novel grasp contact computational model specifically designed to improve the realism of vacuum grasping in NVIDIA Isaac Sim by accurately simulating suction cup seal formation with complex object geometries. The authors integrate their model into Isaac Sim, replacing the default Surface Gripper with a quasi-static spring-based approach that evaluates surface holes and bulges to predict seal integrity. By defining five physical conditions for realistic attachment and detachment behavior, the method significantly reduces the Sim2Real gap, as validated through both simulation and real-world experiments on challenging objects.

- ["Learning Dual-Arm Coordination for Grasping Large Flat Objects" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03500v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03500v1) — This paper presents a model-free deep reinforcement learning framework for dual-arm coordination to grasp large flat objects, leveraging a grasp pose detection model for state representation and a CNN-based PPO algorithm with shared Actor-Critic layers. The system is trained and evaluated in Isaac Gym—a core component of the Isaac Sim ecosystem—and demonstrates successful sim-to-real transfer without fine-tuning, outperforming baseline methods on both seen and unseen objects.

- ["Bench2FreeAD: A Benchmark for Vision-based End-to-end Navigation in Unstructured Robotic Environments" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12180v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.12180v2) — This paper introduces Bench2FreeAD, a benchmark for vision-based end-to-end robot navigation in unstructured environments such as campus paths and indoor settings. It presents the FreeWorld Dataset, combining real-world robot data with synthetic data generated using Isaac Sim, and demonstrates improved navigation performance by fine-tuning the VAD model on this dataset. The work provides the first dedicated benchmark and dataset for E2E navigation in unstructured robotic scenarios, supporting advancements in service and logistics robots.

- ["MarineGym: A High-Performance Reinforcement Learning Platform for Underwater Robotics" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09203v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.09203v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — MarineGym introduces a high-performance reinforcement learning platform for underwater robotics that integrates a custom GPU-accelerated hydrodynamic plugin built on Isaac Sim. By leveraging Isaac Sim’s physics and rendering capabilities, the platform achieves 250,000 frames per second on a single RTX 3060 GPU and includes five UUV models, multiple propulsion systems, and standardized tasks. Its domain randomization toolkit enhances Sim2Real transfer, and benchmark results show superior training efficiency and policy robustness compared to existing simulators, making it a valuable resource for underwater RL research.

- ["Multitask Reinforcement Learning for Quadcopter Attitude Stabilization and Tracking using Graph Policy" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08259v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.08259v1) — The paper proposes a multitask deep reinforcement learning framework for quadcopter attitude control, combining smooth tracking and aggressive stabilization using a Graph Convolutional Network (GCN) policy within a multitask Soft Actor-Critic (SAC) algorithm. It explicitly leverages Isaac Gym for parallelized, high-throughput simulation, enabling efficient training with improved sample efficiency and convergence over single-task baselines. The compact learned policy is successfully deployed on a real Pixhawk flight controller at 400 Hz, demonstrating Isaac Sim’s utility in bridging simulation-to-reality gaps for agile UAV control.

- ["Safe Distributed Learning-Enhanced Predictive Control for Multiple Quadrupedal Robots" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05836v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.05836v1) — This paper presents a distributed model predictive control framework for multi-quadruped formation control that integrates Control Lyapunov and Barrier Functions for stability and safety, along with a novel Scale-Adaptive Permutation-Invariant Encoding (SAPIE) to handle dynamic team structures. The approach is validated using high-fidelity simulations in NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim and real-world tests on the authors' XG quadruped platform, demonstrating robust collision avoidance, real-time coordination, and scalability. The use of Isaac Sim as a core simulation environment underscores its role in validating complex, safety-critical multi-robot behaviors.

- ["Hierarchical Reinforcement Learning for Quadrupedal Robots: Efficient Object Manipulation in Constrained Environments" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4408133349) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1424-8220/25/5/1565/pdf?version=1741072866) (Citations: 1) — Sensors — This paper presents a hierarchical reinforcement learning framework for quadrupedal robots performing object manipulation in cluttered environments, featuring a novel sensor-based reward function that enhances decision-making while reducing computational load. The method was evaluated in NVIDIA Isaac Sim using ANYbotics quadrupedal robots, achieving a mean positioning error of 11 cm over distances up to 10 m and demonstrating efficient path planning and stable operation. The work highlights Isaac Sim as a critical platform for validating complex robotic RL policies under realistic environmental constraints.

- ["Aerial Gym Simulator: A Framework for Highly Parallelized Simulation of Aerial Robots" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01471v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.01471v1) — This paper introduces Aerial Gym Simulator, a highly parallelized framework built on NVIDIA Isaac Gym for simulating diverse multirotor aerial robots. It leverages Isaac Gym’s GPU-accelerated physics to enable massive parallelization of under-, fully-, and over-actuated platforms, and includes custom GPU-based rendering for depth, segmentation, and vertex-level sensor data. The framework supports reinforcement learning for tasks like depth-based navigation and demonstrates successful sim-to-real policy transfer, offering a valuable open-source tool for aerial robotics research.

- ["Runtime Learning of Quadruped Robots in Wild Environments" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04794v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.04794v2) — This paper introduces a runtime learning framework for quadruped robots that combines a deep reinforcement learning agent (HP-Student) with a verifiable physics-based controller (HA-Teacher) to enable safe, adaptive locomotion in dynamic real-world environments. The authors implement and evaluate their approach using the Unitree Go2 robot simulated in NVIDIA Isaac Gym, demonstrating improved safety and performance over existing safe DRL methods. The use of Isaac Gym as the core simulation platform for training and validation makes this work directly relevant to the Isaac Sim ecosystem.

- ["Multi-Keypoint Affordance Representation for Functional Dexterous Grasping" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20018v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.20018v2) — The paper proposes a multi-keypoint affordance representation for functional dexterous grasping, introducing Contact-guided Multi-Keypoint Affordance (CMKA) and Keypoint-based Grasp matrix Transformation (KGT) to link visual perception with precise hand-object interactions. It explicitly uses IsaacGym simulation for evaluation alongside real-world datasets, demonstrating improved grasp consistency and generalization. The integration of IsaacGym as an experimental platform validates the method’s performance in simulated dexterous manipulation tasks, offering practical value for robotic grasping research.

- ["Wheeled Lab: Modern Sim2Real for Low-cost, Open-source Wheeled Robotics" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07380v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.07380v2) — The paper introduces Wheeled Lab, an open-source ecosystem that integrates low-cost wheeled robots with Isaac Lab—a widely adopted open-source robot learning and simulation framework—to enable accessible Sim2Real reinforcement learning for education and research. Using Isaac Lab as the core simulation and training platform, the authors demonstrate zero-shot transfer of three complex policies (controlled drifting, elevation traversal, and visual navigation) to real RC cars. The full hardware-software stack is open-source and affordable, significantly lowering barriers to entry for teaching and experimenting with modern robotics.

- ["Value-Based Deep RL Scales Predictably" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04327v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.04327v2) — This paper demonstrates that value-based off-policy reinforcement learning methods exhibit predictable scaling behavior when increasing data and compute resources. The authors identify a Pareto frontier governed by the updates-to-data (UTD) ratio, enabling prediction of performance and optimal resource allocation across data and compute. They validate their findings using SAC, BRO, and PQL on multiple benchmarks, including Isaac Gym—a GPU-accelerated RL environment within the Isaac Sim ecosystem—showing reliable extrapolation to larger scales.

- ["ASAP: Aligning Simulation and Real-World Physics for Learning Agile Humanoid Whole-Body Skills" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01143v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.01143v3) — The paper introduces ASAP, a two-stage framework that aligns simulation and real-world physics to enable agile humanoid whole-body skills by compensating for dynamics mismatch. It explicitly uses Isaac Sim as a key transfer target in one of its evaluation scenarios (IsaacGym to IsaacSim), demonstrating how policies trained in IsaacGym are refined using real-world data and then validated within Isaac Sim to improve fidelity and agility. This work highlights Isaac Sim’s role as a high-fidelity simulation environment for transferring and validating agile humanoid control policies.

- ["Real Case Studies in Industry 5.0: The Example of Nvidia" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4406825094) (Citations: 1) — This qualitative study explores Nvidia's role in advancing Industry 5.0 through key technologies, including its Isaac platform for humanoid robot development. It highlights Isaac Sim as a tool for realistic simulation to enable safe human–robot collaboration, alongside GR00T for behavior imitation and Jetson Thor for processing. While the paper mentions Isaac Sim in the context of Nvidia’s robotics ecosystem, it does not present original research, experiments, or technical contributions centered on Isaac Sim itself.

- ["Sim-to-Real Transfer for Mobile Robots with Reinforcement Learning: from NVIDIA Isaac Sim to Gazebo and Real ROS 2 Robots" (2025)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2501.02902v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.02902v1) (Citations: 2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a framework for training end-to-end reinforcement learning policies for mobile robot local navigation using NVIDIA Isaac Sim, focusing on exteroception-based obstacle avoidance and sim-to-real transfer. The authors demonstrate zero-shot policy transfer from Isaac Sim to real ROS 2 robots and validate generalization across different simulated robot platforms, achieving performance comparable to the Nav2 stack. By leveraging Isaac Sim as the core training environment, the work provides a reproducible pipeline for developing and deploying agile navigation policies on custom mobile robots.

- ["Choosing the Arena: A Systematic Review of Simulators for Deep Reinforcement Learning in Mobile Robot Navigation" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W7118007974) [PDF](https://thesai.org/Downloads/Volume16No12/Paper_62-Choosing_the_Arena_A_Systematic_Review_of_Simulators.pdf) — International Journal of Advanced Computer Science and Applications — This systematic literature review analyzes 87 studies to provide an evidence-based framework for selecting simulators in deep reinforcement learning for mobile robot navigation. It classifies simulators into three archetypes, identifying NVIDIA Isaac Sim as a representative of Archetype III—GPU-native engines optimized for large-scale, perception-rich tasks that enable photorealistic rendering and zero-shot sim-to-real transfer. The work elevates simulator choice from a technical afterthought to a strategic research decision.

- ["Isaac Sim Integrated Digital Twin for Feasibility Checks in Skill-Based Engineering" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4413869787) — Mechanisms and machine science — This paper presents an integrated digital twin framework using Isaac Sim to perform feasibility checks in skill-based engineering workflows. The authors leverage Isaac Sim's high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated physics simulation to validate robotic skills and task sequences before real-world deployment, ensuring consistency between virtual and physical robot behaviors. By embedding domain-specific engineering skills into the simulation environment, the system enables rapid prototyping and verification of complex robotic operations, significantly reducing development time and hardware wear.

- ["Automatic Docking of Modules of Modular Robots: Development and Evaluation of an Automatic Docking Method in Isaac Sim" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W7115909426) [PDF](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/jrsj/43/10/43_43_1012/_pdf) — Journal of the Robotics Society of Japan — This paper presents a novel automatic docking method for modular robots, specifically developed and evaluated within NVIDIA's Isaac Sim simulation environment. The authors leverage Isaac Sim's high-fidelity physics and GPU-accelerated capabilities to simulate precise alignment and connection mechanisms between robot modules, enabling robust testing of docking strategies under realistic conditions. The work demonstrates how Isaac Sim serves as a critical platform for prototyping and validating complex robotic behaviors before real-world deployment.

- ["Adaptive Multi-Robot Exploration for Unknown Environments Using Edge-Weighted Path Planning" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4411472236) (Citations: 1) — IEEE Access — This paper proposes a scalable, adaptive multi-robot exploration algorithm that dynamically updates edge weights using visit counts, reservations, and obstacle information to optimize path planning and ensure full area coverage. The method was validated in both 2D simulations and a high-fidelity 3D environment using Isaac Sim with ROS integration, demonstrating improved exploration efficiency and real-time adaptability over baseline approaches. The use of Isaac Sim as a realistic testing platform underscores its value for evaluating autonomous multi-robot systems in complex, unknown environments.

- ["Realistic Simulation of Urban Air Mobility Scenarios" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W7126737649) — This paper introduces a simulation tool for Urban Air Mobility (UAM) scenarios compliant with EU U-space regulations, designed to model dense UAV traffic in urban environments. Built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim, the platform leverages its high-fidelity physics and parallel simulation capabilities to enable realistic, scalable testing of airspace management systems, reducing real-world deployment risks and development costs. The integration with Isaac Sim provides hyper-realistic environmental rendering and efficient experiment analysis for complex multi-UAV coordination.

- ["Digital Twin-Enabled Robotic Automation of Electrolyzer Assemblies for Power-to-X Solutions" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4413882120) — Mechanisms and machine science — This paper proposes a digital twin-enabled robotic automation framework for assembling electrolyzers in Power-to-X systems. While it discusses simulation-based development and virtual commissioning, the work does not explicitly mention or utilize Isaac Sim, Isaac Lab, Isaac Gym, or Nvidia Newton as the simulation platform or core methodology. The focus is on digital twin architecture and robotic control rather than leveraging NVIDIA's Isaac ecosystem.

- ["ORB-SLAM2 System Enhanced With SiaT-Hough Module on Mars Digital Twin Platform: For Rock Counting and 3D Reconstruction in Martian Environments" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4412030352) — IEEE Access — This paper presents a Mars digital twin platform built using NVIDIA Isaac Sim to generate realistic, multi-scale Martian terrains for robotic exploration tasks. The authors enhance the ORB-SLAM2 visual-inertial odometry system with a novel SiaT-Hough module—combining a lightweight Siamese Transformer and Hough transform—for rock segmentation, counting, and 3D reconstruction in simulated Martian environments. Evaluated on a custom Mars simulation dataset generated in Isaac Sim, the system achieves high segmentation accuracy (87.86%), real-time performance (57.34 FPS), and precise 3D localization, demonstrating Isaac Sim’s utility as a foundational platform for extraterrestrial robotics research.

- ["Performance Analysis of a Mass-Spring-Damper Deformable Linear Object Model in Robotic Simulation Frameworks" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4410514945) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.13659) — Springer proceedings in advanced robotics — This paper evaluates the performance of a mass-spring-damper model for simulating deformable linear objects (e.g., cables, ropes) across multiple robotic simulation frameworks. While it compares several platforms, Isaac Sim is not explicitly used as a core simulation environment or analyzed in depth; it may only be mentioned peripherally, if at all. The study focuses on accuracy, computational efficiency, and fidelity of deformable object dynamics rather than contributions to or within Isaac Sim itself.

- ["GOME-NGU: Visual Navigation Under Sparse Reward via Goal-Oriented Memory Encoder With Never Give Up" (2025)](https://openalex.org/W4409049667) — IEEE Access — The paper introduces GOME-NGU, a visual navigation method combining the Never Give Up (NGU) exploration algorithm with a Goal-Oriented Memory Encoder (GOME) to improve performance in sparse-reward settings. Training was conducted in NVIDIA Isaac Gym, and validation was performed by migrating policies to Isaac Sim, including experiments with a Husky robot to demonstrate effective path optimization toward nearby goals. This work highlights Isaac Sim’s role as a high-fidelity validation platform for sim-to-sim transfer in robotic navigation.

- ["Learning an Adaptive Fall Recovery Controller for Quadrupeds on Complex Terrains" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16924v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.16924v1) — This paper introduces an Adaptive Fall Recovery (AFR) controller for quadrupedal robots using deep reinforcement learning, trained specifically in Isaac Gym—a component of the Isaac Sim ecosystem. The method enables robust recovery on complex terrains like rocky surfaces and steep slopes, and is validated through direct transfer to real-world platforms such as Spot and ANYmal. By leveraging Isaac Gym’s GPU-accelerated simulation, the approach demonstrates high success rates and fast recovery times, offering practical value for real-world legged locomotion systems.

- ["RoboMIND: Benchmark on Multi-embodiment Intelligence Normative Data for Robot Manipulation" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13877v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.13877v3) (Citations: 2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — RoboMIND introduces a large-scale dataset of 107k robot manipulation trajectories across 479 tasks and four robotic embodiments, collected via human teleoperation with multi-view observations, proprioceptive states, and linguistic descriptions. The authors created a digital twin of their real-world setup in Isaac Sim to enable low-cost synthetic data generation and efficient policy evaluation, directly leveraging Isaac Sim as a core component for simulation-based training and benchmarking.

- ["АУТОНОМНА НАВИГАЦИЈА РОБОТА МЕЂУ РЕДОВИМА ПОЉОПРИВРЕДНИХ ЗАСАДА" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4405171754) [PDF](https://www.ftn.uns.ac.rs/ojs/index.php/zbornik/article/download/3733/3349) — Zbornik radova Fakulteta tehničkih nauka u Novom Sadu — This paper presents a system for autonomous navigation of an unmanned ground vehicle (UGV) among rows of agricultural crops, comparing traditional image processing with machine learning approaches for row tracking. The authors used NVIDIA Isaac Sim to generate semantic datasets for training a YOLOv8 model and tested their full navigation pipeline—including RTK-GPS for end-of-row detection and a ROS 2-based NAV2 Waypoint Follower using Pure Pursuit—in both simulation and real-world environments. The work demonstrates Isaac Sim’s utility in creating labeled synthetic data for agricultural robotics tasks.

- ["InfiniteWorld: A Unified Scalable Simulation Framework for General Visual-Language Robot Interaction" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05789v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.05789v1) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — InfiniteWorld introduces a unified and scalable simulation framework for general vision-language robot interaction built directly on NVIDIA Isaac Sim. The work presents integrated advancements in generation-driven 3D asset construction, Real2Sim transfer, automated annotation, and standardized asset processing, enabling consistent and large-scale embodied AI research. It also proposes four novel benchmarks—including scene graph collaborative exploration and open-world social mobile manipulation—to evaluate agents’ environmental understanding, planning, and interactive capabilities within Isaac Sim’s physics-rich environment.

- ["NaVILA: Legged Robot Vision-Language-Action Model for Navigation" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04453v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.04453v2) — NaVILA introduces a two-level Vision-Language-Action framework for legged robot navigation, where high-level language instructions are first converted into mid-level spatial commands (e.g., 'move forward 75cm'), which are then executed by a visual reinforcement learning locomotion policy. The method is evaluated on newly developed benchmarks built in Isaac Lab, which offer realistic environments, low-level control interfaces, and alignment with real-world robot experiments. This work demonstrates Isaac Sim's utility as a platform for developing and validating complex embodied AI systems that bridge language, vision, and action.

- ["Learning Dual-Arm Push and Grasp Synergy in Dense Clutter" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04052v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.04052v2) — The paper proposes a hierarchical deep reinforcement learning framework that learns synergistic dual-arm push-grasp strategies for object retrieval in dense clutter. It leverages a pre-trained visual backbone and a novel CNN-based PPO-trained policy with a fuzzy reward function to map RGB observations to coordinated 6-DoF dual-arm actions. The system is trained entirely in Isaac Gym, demonstrating successful sim-to-real transfer on a physical robot, and outperforms baseline methods by enabling human-like push-grasp coordination in highly cluttered scenes.

- ["Robotic Path Planning Algorithms for Additive Manufacturing Using Advanced Simulation Tools" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W7123379658) [PDF](https://iconline.ipleiria.pt/bitstreams/c40ee2aa-2645-4fb2-ab4f-e2d53f631f8b/download) — IC-Online (Scientific Information of the Polytechnic Institute of Leiria) — This paper evaluates robotic path planning algorithms—specifically MotionGen and Model Predictive Control (MPC)—for additive manufacturing in dynamic environments, using NVIDIA’s Isaac Sim integrated with ROS2 and MoveIt2. The study leverages the cuRobo library within Isaac Sim to simulate and compare trajectory efficiency, finding that MotionGen produces smoother, more energy- and time-efficient paths than MPC. Real-time obstacle detection is enabled via an Intel RealSense camera, and Docker ensures a reproducible setup. The work demonstrates Isaac Sim’s utility as a high-fidelity simulation platform for developing and benchmarking advanced robotic control strategies tailored to real-time 3D printing applications.

- ["Bimanual Grasp Synthesis for Dexterous Robot Hands" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15903v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.15903v1) — The paper introduces BimanGrasp, an algorithm for synthesizing stable and feasible bimanual grasp poses for dexterous robot hands by optimizing an energy function. It leverages Isaac Gym—a component of the Isaac Sim ecosystem—for physics-based verification of generated grasps, resulting in the BimanGrasp-Dataset, which contains over 150k verified grasps on 900 objects. The authors also present BimanGrasp-DDPM, a diffusion model trained on this dataset that achieves a 69.87% success rate and faster grasp synthesis, demonstrating the value of Isaac Sim as a critical validation platform for large-scale robotic manipulation research.

- ["A Benchmark Dataset for Collaborative SLAM in Service Environments" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2411.14775v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.14775v1) (Citations: 2) — IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters — This paper introduces CSE, a novel benchmark dataset for collaborative SLAM (C-SLAM) in diverse indoor service environments such as hospitals, offices, and warehouses. The authors leverage NVIDIA Isaac Sim to generate realistic, multi-modal, and time-synchronized sensor data—including stereo RGB, stereo depth, IMU, and ground truth poses—while simulating dynamic objects and coordinated motion of three service robots. By using Isaac Sim, they ensure high-fidelity physics and precise control over environmental challenges, enabling robust evaluation of C-SLAM algorithms under realistic conditions.

- ["A systemic survey of the Omniverse platform and its applications in data generation, simulation and metaverse" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4404509220) (Citations: 4) — Frontiers in Computer Science — This paper provides a systematic survey of NVIDIA's Omniverse platform, with a dedicated focus on Isaac Sim—specifically Isaac Gym and its SDK—as a core tool for robotic simulation. It examines how Isaac Sim contributes to data generation, physics-based simulation, and AI training within the broader Omniverse ecosystem, alongside applications like Drive Sim and Replicator. The work contextualizes Isaac Sim’s role in advancing robotics research and highlights real-world usage patterns from existing literature.

- ["Utilisation of Vision Systems and Digital Twin for Maintaining Cleanliness in Public Spaces" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05964v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.05964v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a cleanliness management system for public spaces that integrates vision-based surveillance and Digital Twin technology, using a railway station as a case study. The authors built a virtual replica of the station in NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim to simulate and test detection algorithms—including litter detection, bin occupancy monitoring, stain segmentation, and human (including cleaning staff) tracking and movement analysis—before real-world deployment. By leveraging Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform, the work demonstrates how GPU-accelerated digital twins can optimize cleaning operations and validate AI-driven perception systems in complex environments.

- ["TacEx: GelSight Tactile Simulation in Isaac Sim -- Combining Soft-Body and Visuotactile Simulators" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04776v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.04776v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — TacEx introduces a modular tactile simulation framework that integrates the soft-body contact simulator GIPC and vision-based tactile simulators Taxim and FOTS into Isaac Sim to accurately simulate the GelSight Mini visuotactile sensor. The authors implement multiple Isaac Lab environments for reinforcement learning—such as object pushing, lifting, and pole balancing—and demonstrate that high-dimensional tactile observations like gel deformation and RGB images are stable and effective for policy training. This work significantly enhances Isaac Sim’s capabilities for contact-rich manipulation tasks by providing a reliable, open-source tactile simulation pipeline.

- ["Learning Generalizable Policy for Obstacle-Aware Autonomous Drone Racing" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04246v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.04246v1) — This paper presents a deep reinforcement learning approach for learning generalizable obstacle-aware drone racing policies by leveraging domain randomization of racing tracks and obstacle configurations. The authors use Isaac Gym (part of the Isaac Sim ecosystem) for massively parallel simulation, enabling efficient training at high speeds (up to 70 km/h) in randomized cluttered environments. Their method demonstrates strong zero-shot transfer to unseen tracks, advancing robust autonomous navigation in complex settings.

- ["Evaluating PyBullet and Isaac Sim in the Scope of Robotics and Reinforcement Learning" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4405709846) (Citations: 4) — This paper conducts a comparative evaluation of PyBullet and Isaac Sim as simulation platforms for robotics and reinforcement learning, analyzing their performance, features, and suitability for different tasks. While it discusses Isaac Sim in detail as one of the two main platforms under comparison, the work does not focus exclusively on Isaac Sim nor introduce new methods or datasets built specifically for it. The study provides practical insights for researchers choosing between these simulators but treats Isaac Sim as one option among others rather than as the central contribution.

- ["The Role of Domain Randomization in Training Diffusion Policies for Whole-Body Humanoid Control" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01349v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.01349v1) — This paper investigates how dataset diversity and size impact the training of Diffusion Policies (DPs) for whole-body humanoid locomotion, a domain where DPs have been less explored compared to manipulation. The authors generate synthetic demonstrations in Isaac Gym by training Adversarial Motion Prior (AMP) agents under varying Domain Randomization conditions, then fit DPs to datasets of differing scale and diversity. Their results show that stable walking policies require substantially larger and more diverse datasets than typical manipulation tasks, highlighting key challenges in applying diffusion models to humanoid control within the Isaac Sim ecosystem.

- ["In-Simulation Testing of Deep Learning Vision Models in Autonomous Robotic Manipulators" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19277v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.19277v2) (Citations: 2) — The paper introduces MARTENS, a framework that integrates NVIDIA Isaac Sim with evolutionary search to test and improve deep learning vision models for autonomous robotic manipulators. By generating critical scenarios in Isaac Sim's photorealistic environment, MARTENS identifies system failures and design flaws, enabling targeted model repair and enhancement. Evaluated on two industrial case studies, the approach detects 25–50% more diverse failures than random testing and achieves high real-world mAP scores (0.91 and 0.82) without prior real data, demonstrating Isaac Sim’s value in accelerating vision model development and validation.

- ["NavRL: Learning Safe Flight in Dynamic Environments" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15634v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.15634v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces NavRL, a deep reinforcement learning framework for safe UAV navigation in dynamic environments using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO). It leverages NVIDIA Isaac Sim to enable highly parallelized training with thousands of simulated quadcopters, significantly accelerating policy convergence. The method incorporates a velocity-obstacle-inspired safety shield and demonstrates zero-shot sim-to-real transfer, validated through both simulation and real-world flight tests.

- [" High fidelity Mars Rover simulation platform based on digital twin and machine learning integration: Innovation and application of ISMRS " (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4402359048) [PDF](https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-4851864/latest.pdf) — The paper introduces the Isaac Sim Martian Robotic Simulator (ISMRS), a high-fidelity digital twin platform built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim for Mars rover development. It leverages Isaac Sim to generate realistic Martian terrains, supports ROS2-based autonomous navigation, and integrates synthetic data training with YOLOv8 for rock segmentation—demonstrating strong sim-to-real transfer. The work validates Isaac Sim’s capability as a core simulation environment for extraterrestrial robotics and machine learning pipelines.

- ["Learning to Singulate Objects in Packed Environments using a Dexterous Hand" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00643v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.00643v2) — The paper introduces the SOPE framework for robotic object singulation in tightly packed clutter using a dexterous 16-DOF Allegro Hand. It proposes a displacement-based state representation and a multi-phase reinforcement learning approach, trained and extensively evaluated in Isaac Gym simulation. The policy transfers directly to real-world hardware, achieving a 79.2% success rate over 250 physical trials, outperforming baselines.

- ["Advancing Humanoid Locomotion: Mastering Challenging Terrains with Denoising World Model Learning" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4402703212) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.14472) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces Denoising World Model Learning (DWL), an end-to-end reinforcement learning framework that enables a humanoid robot to navigate highly challenging real-world terrains—including snow, stairs, and uneven ground—using a single policy with zero-shot sim-to-real transfer. However, the abstract does not mention Isaac Sim, Isaac Gym, Isaac Lab, or any NVIDIA simulation platform as the training environment or a core component of the method.

- ["TacSL: A Library for Visuotactile Sensor Simulation and Learning" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4402426888) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.06506) — arXiv (Cornell University) — TacSL is a GPU-accelerated library for simulating visuotactile sensors and learning tactile-based policies, achieving over 200× speedup in generating visuotactile images and contact-force distributions compared to prior methods. It is explicitly built within the Isaac Simulator (Isaac Sim), providing sensor models, contact-rich training environments, and learning algorithms—including a novel online reinforcement learning method called asymmetric actor-critic distillation (AACD)—to enable efficient sim-to-real policy transfer. This work directly leverages Isaac Sim as its core simulation platform, making it highly relevant for researchers using NVIDIA’s ecosystem for tactile robotics.

- ["Learning to Walk with Adaptive Feet" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4400941985) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2218-6581/13/8/113/pdf?version=1721986738) (Citations: 2) — Robotics — This paper investigates end-to-end reinforcement learning policies for quadrupedal locomotion that leverage terrain-sensing adaptive feet to improve dynamic walking performance. However, the abstract does not mention Isaac Sim, Isaac Gym, Isaac Lab, or any NVIDIA simulation platform as the training or experimental environment. Without explicit use of or contribution to Isaac Sim, the work remains a general RL-based locomotion study.

- ["Random Latent Exploration for Deep Reinforcement Learning" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13755v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.13755v3) — The paper introduces Random Latent Exploration (RLE), a plug-and-play exploration strategy for deep reinforcement learning that samples random goals in a latent space to encourage diverse behavior. While the method is evaluated across multiple benchmarks including Atari and continuous control tasks, it only uses Isaac Gym as one of several standard environments for testing—without focusing on Isaac Sim as a core contribution, platform, or subject of study. The work does not develop or modify Isaac Sim itself, nor is it centered on its unique capabilities.

- ["Autonomous robotic 3D scanning for smart factory planning" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4399423245) (Citations: 2) — This paper proposes an autonomous mobile ground robotic system for 3D scanning in smart factory planning, aiming to reduce human intervention and improve precision in large-scale industrial environments. The authors use NVIDIA Isaac Sim as a simulation platform to develop, prototype, and test their robotic scanning framework, demonstrating its utility for validating autonomous scanning strategies before real-world deployment. The work highlights Isaac Sim’s role in accelerating the design cycle for industrial robotics applications.

- ["Soft body simulation of fish in fish processing factories" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4400949143) [PDF](http://www.scs-europe.net/dlib/2024/ecms2024acceptedpapers/0150_dtsm_ecms2024_0021.pdf) — This paper investigates the use of NVIDIA Isaac Sim within the Omniverse platform to simulate soft-body dynamics of fish in a fish processing factory environment. The authors develop a digital twin of a physical fish processing line, leveraging Isaac Sim's physics capabilities to model realistic deformable interactions between fish and machinery. The simulation is validated against real-world production data, demonstrating its potential as a decision-support tool for optimizing industrial fish processing systems.

- ["A Robust Strategy for UAV Autonomous Landing on a Moving Platform under Partial Observability" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4399173563) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2504-446X/8/6/232/pdf?version=1717064560) (Citations: 8) — Drones — The paper proposes a deep reinforcement learning algorithm called RPO-LSTM, which integrates LSTM networks into a modified PPO architecture to enable robust autonomous UAV landing on moving platforms under partial observability. While the method is evaluated in simulation environments, the abstract does not mention Isaac Sim, Isaac Lab, Isaac Gym, or Nvidia Newton as the simulation platform. The work focuses on algorithmic innovation rather than leveraging or contributing to the Isaac Sim ecosystem.

- ["Learning Quadruped Locomotion Policies Using Logical Rules" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4399176222) [PDF](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/ICAPS/article/download/31470/33630) (Citations: 3) — Proceedings of the International Conference on Automated Planning and Scheduling — This paper introduces RM-based Locomotion Learning (RMLL), a method that uses Reward Machines and logical rules to specify quadruped gaits without relying on motion priors or manual tuning. While the approach is evaluated in simulation and deployed on a real robot, the abstract and available information do not indicate that Isaac Sim was used as the simulation environment or that the work contributes to or focuses on Isaac Sim in any way.

- ["Maximum Entropy Reinforcement Learning via Energy-Based Normalizing Flow" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13629v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.13629v2) — This paper proposes EBFlow, a Maximum Entropy Reinforcement Learning framework that unifies policy evaluation and improvement into a single objective using Energy-Based Normalizing Flows. It enables exact computation of the soft value function without Monte Carlo approximation and supports multi-modal action distributions with efficient sampling. The method is evaluated on MuJoCo and high-dimensional robotic tasks in Omniverse Isaac Gym, showing superior performance over standard baselines.

- ["Going into Orbit: Massively Parallelizing Episodic Reinforcement Learning" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11512v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.11512v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a detailed implementation and benchmarking of a box-pushing reinforcement learning task using NVIDIA's Orbit framework, which is built on Isaac Sim for GPU-accelerated simulation. The authors demonstrate significant gains in sampling efficiency by leveraging Orbit’s tensor-based, massively parallel architecture compared to a CPU-based baseline, and they provide hyperparameter tuning insights that maximize sample throughput. Their work highlights Isaac Sim’s role as a foundational simulation platform enabling high-performance, scalable robot learning.

- ["Task and Domain Adaptive Reinforcement Learning for Robot Control" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18713v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.18713v3) — This paper introduces a novel adaptive reinforcement learning agent that uses transfer learning to dynamically adjust policies across tasks and environmental conditions, specifically targeting robot control challenges. The method is developed and trained using a custom, highly parallelized simulator built on Isaac Gym, enabling efficient multi-task training for a blimp controller. The approach demonstrates successful zero-shot transfer to real-world blimp operation, solving diverse tasks without fine-tuning, highlighting Isaac Sim’s role in bridging simulation and reality.

- ["Humanoid-Gym: Reinforcement Learning for Humanoid Robot with Zero-Shot Sim2Real Transfer" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05695v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.05695v2) (Citations: 5) — arXiv (Cornell University) — Humanoid-Gym is a reinforcement learning framework built on NVIDIA Isaac Gym that enables efficient training of humanoid locomotion policies with zero-shot sim-to-real transfer. It leverages Isaac Gym's GPU-accelerated simulation to train robust policies and includes a sim-to-sim evaluation pipeline from Isaac Gym to MuJoCo to test generalization. The framework was validated on RobotEra's XBot-S and XBot-L humanoid robots, demonstrating successful real-world deployment without fine-tuning.

- ["Benchmarking Population-Based Reinforcement Learning across Robotic Tasks with GPU-Accelerated Simulation" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03336v5) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.03336v5) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces a Population-Based Reinforcement Learning (PBRL) framework that leverages GPU-accelerated simulation in Isaac Gym to improve exploration and hyperparameter adaptation across robotic tasks. The authors benchmark PBRL against PPO, SAC, and DDPG on four challenging environments—Anymal Terrain, Shadow Hand, Humanoid, and Franka Nut Pick—demonstrating superior cumulative reward performance and successful sim-to-real transfer on the Franka robot. By exploiting Isaac Gym’s parallelized physics simulation, the work highlights the efficiency gains of population-based methods in large-scale RL training and real-world deployment.

- ["Adaptive Energy Regularization for Autonomous Gait Transition and Energy-Efficient Quadruped Locomotion" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20001v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.20001v2) — This paper proposes an adaptive energy-based reward strategy for reinforcement learning that enables quadruped robots like ANYmal-C and Unitree Go1 to autonomously transition between energy-efficient gaits (e.g., walking at low speeds, trotting at higher speeds) without relying on predefined gait patterns. The method is trained and validated in the Isaac Gym simulation environment—a core component of NVIDIA's Isaac Sim ecosystem—and demonstrates improved energy efficiency and velocity tracking compared to complex reward designs. The successful sim-to-real transfer highlights Isaac Gym’s utility for scalable, physics-accurate legged locomotion research.

- ["Arm-Constrained Curriculum Learning for Loco-Manipulation of the Wheel-Legged Robot" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16535v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.16535v2) — This paper introduces an arm-constrained curriculum learning framework for loco-manipulation tasks on a wheel-legged robot equipped with a manipulator. The authors develop a reinforcement learning algorithm with arm constraints and a reward-aware curriculum to balance base and arm control objectives. Training is conducted in Isaac Gym, and the policy is successfully transferred to a real robot for dynamic grasping tasks such as door opening and catching moving objects. The work demonstrates Isaac Sim’s utility as a high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated training environment for complex mobile manipulation policies.

- ["Digital Twin and Deep Reinforcement Learning-Driven Robotic Automation System for Confined Workspaces: A Nozzle Dam Replacement Case Study in Nuclear Power Plants" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4392914637) [PDF](https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s40684-023-00593-6.pdf) (Citations: 16) — International Journal of Precision Engineering and Manufacturing-Green Technology — This paper presents a robotic automation system for nozzle dam replacement in nuclear power plants, combining a high-fidelity digital twin with deep reinforcement learning. The authors implement their digital twin using NVIDIA Isaac Sim to simulate a confined workspace and train an autonomous mobile manipulator via proximal policy optimization, enhanced with an inverse reachability map and centroidal waypoint strategy. The system integrates hybrid visual perception for 6D pose estimation and validates performance through both simulation and real-world testing, demonstrating a practical pipeline for deploying robots in hazardous, constrained environments.

- ["MultiGripperGrasp: A Dataset for Robotic Grasping from Parallel Jaw Grippers to Dexterous Hands" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09841v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.09841v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces MultiGripperGrasp, a large-scale robotic grasping dataset containing 30.4 million grasps across 11 grippers and 345 objects, ranging from parallel-jaw to dexterous five-finger hands. All grasps are validated in NVIDIA's Isaac Sim simulator to label success/failure and record object fall-off time as a grasp quality metric. The palm-aligned gripper design enables cross-gripper grasp transfer, significantly boosting successful grasp counts. This dataset supports research in generalized grasp planning and transfer learning across diverse end-effectors, with practical value for training and benchmarking in simulation-based robotics.

- ["Sim-to-Real gap in RL: Use Case with TIAGo and Isaac Sim/Gym" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07091v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.07091v2) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper investigates sim-to-real transfer for robotic manipulation using the TIAGo mobile manipulator, directly comparing NVIDIA's Isaac Gym and Isaac Sim as core simulation platforms for reinforcement learning. The authors develop and evaluate control architectures aimed at achieving collision-free motion, demonstrating successful policy transfer from both simulators to the real robot with highly consistent behaviors. Their work provides practical insights into the relative strengths of Isaac Sim and Isaac Gym for realistic, deployable RL policies in mobile manipulation tasks.

- ["GenNBV: Generalizable Next-Best-View Policy for Active 3D Reconstruction" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16174v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.16174v3) — GenNBV introduces an end-to-end, generalizable Next-Best-View (NBV) policy for active 3D reconstruction using reinforcement learning, operating in a 5D continuous action space to enable flexible drone-based scanning of unseen geometries. The method leverages a novel multi-source state embedding combining geometric, semantic, and action representations to enhance cross-dataset generalization. Evaluated in the Isaac Gym simulator using Houses3K and OmniObject3D datasets, GenNBV achieves over 97% coverage on unseen building-scale objects, significantly outperforming prior NBV approaches.

- ["Tiny Reinforcement Learning for Quadruped Locomotion using Decision Transformers" (2024)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13201v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.13201v1) — This paper proposes a method to deploy imitation learning on resource-constrained quadruped robots by framing it as a conditional sequence modeling task using a Decision Transformer trained on expert demonstrations with a custom reward. The model is compressed via quantization and pruning to reduce size while maintaining performance. Experiments are conducted in Isaac Gym, where the approach achieves natural gaits for the Bittle robot and demonstrates that 4-bit quantization and pruning yield ~30% model size reduction with minimal performance loss, enabling deployment on low-cost hardware.

- ["<i>OmniDrones:</i> An Efficient and Flexible Platform for Reinforcement Learning in Drone Control" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4391019623) (Citations: 33) — IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters — The paper introduces OmniDrones, an open-source reinforcement learning platform specifically designed for drone control, built directly on NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim. It leverages Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics and rendering to provide 4 drone models, 5 sensor types, 4 control modes, and over 10 benchmark tasks—from basic hovering to complex over-actuated tracking—along with integrated RL baselines. By offering a modular, bottom-up framework for designing drone scenarios, OmniDrones enables efficient experimentation and aims to advance practical RL applications in aerial robotics.

- ["A Multiarm Robotic Platform for Scientific Exploration: Its Design, Digital Twins, and Validation" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4390874093) [PDF](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/ielx7/100/4600619/10399868.pdf) (Citations: 11) — IEEE Robotics & Automation Magazine — This paper introduces a multiarm robotic platform designed for scientific exploration, particularly in biomedical research involving organoids and cranial window preparation in mice. The authors develop digital twins of the platform and validate its dexterity through teleoperated tasks such as peg transfer, gauze cutting, egg manipulation, and the first-ever two-operator teleoperated cranial drilling. However, the work does not mention or utilize Isaac Sim, Isaac Lab, Isaac Gym, or Nvidia Newton in its methodology, simulation framework, or experimental validation.

- ["Visual-Language Decision System Through Integration of Foundation Models for Service Robot Navigation" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4391695313) (Citations: 5) — This paper proposes a visual-language decision (VLD) system that integrates multiple foundation models—CLIP, OFA, PaddleOCR, and GPT-3—to enable service robots to interpret their surroundings and make navigation decisions in unexplored environments. The authors implement a photorealistic simulation environment using Isaac Sim to test and validate the VLD system before deploying it on a real TurtleBot3 robot. The work demonstrates how Isaac Sim facilitates realistic training and evaluation of complex perception-decision pipelines for robotic navigation.

- ["General-Purpose Sim2Real Protocol for Learning Contact-Rich Manipulation With Marker-Based Visuotactile Sensors" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4390776907) [PDF](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/ielx7/8860/4359257/10388459.pdf) (Citations: 18) — IEEE Transactions on Robotics — This paper proposes a general-purpose Sim2Real protocol for contact-rich manipulation using marker-based visuotactile sensors, featuring an FEM-based physics simulator for accurate tactile deformation modeling and a novel tactile feature extraction network that processes marker pixel coordinates directly. Although the work emphasizes high-fidelity simulation and policy learning, it does not mention Isaac Sim as the simulation platform or integrate with NVIDIA's ecosystem; instead, it uses a custom FEM-based simulator. The open-sourced protocol offers a valuable framework for visuotactile Sim2Real research but lacks direct relevance to Isaac Sim.

- ["Automation in Unstructured Production Environments Using Isaac Sim: A Flexible Framework for Dynamic Robot Adaptability" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4404789713) (Citations: 7) — Procedia CIRP — This paper presents a flexible framework for enabling robotic systems to operate autonomously in unstructured production environments by leveraging NVIDIA Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform. The authors utilize Isaac Sim's GPU-accelerated physics and sensor simulation capabilities to develop and test dynamic adaptation strategies, including real-time perception, task replanning, and policy transfer. By integrating domain randomization and synthetic data generation within Isaac Sim, the framework enhances robot robustness and generalization in unpredictable industrial settings, offering a scalable approach for deploying adaptable automation solutions.

- ["Stabilization of a Quintuple Inverted Pendulum System in Isaac Sim" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4405821495) — Lecture notes on data engineering and communications technologies — This paper presents a control strategy for stabilizing a quintuple inverted pendulum—a highly unstable nonlinear system—using NVIDIA's Isaac Sim as the primary simulation environment. The authors implement and validate their controller within Isaac Sim's GPU-accelerated physics engine, leveraging its high-fidelity multi-body dynamics to test real-time stabilization performance. The work demonstrates Isaac Sim's capability to handle complex, high-DOF robotic control problems with precision and efficiency.

- ["Enhancement of Control Performance for Degraded Robot Manipulators Using Digital Twin and Proximal Policy Optimization" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4391305596) [PDF](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/ielx7/6287639/6514899/10415386.pdf) (Citations: 7) — IEEE Access — This paper proposes a method to enhance control performance of degraded robot manipulators by combining digital twin modeling and Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO). The authors create a digital twin in a physics engine, tune its parameters to match the degraded robot's unstable dynamics, and apply domain randomization during PPO training to improve policy robustness. The trained policy is deployed on a real six-DOF manipulator, reducing position error by up to 63% compared to baseline controllers. While the work uses a physics-based simulator, it does not explicitly mention or utilize Isaac Sim, Isaac Gym, Isaac Lab, or Nvidia Newton.

- ["Sim-to-Real Gap in RL: Use Case with TIAGo and Isaac Sim/Gym" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W4405927045) (Citations: 2) — Springer proceedings in advanced robotics — This paper investigates the sim-to-real transfer gap in reinforcement learning using the TIAGo robot platform, with simulations conducted in Isaac Sim and Isaac Gym. The authors analyze discrepancies between simulated and real-world performance, proposing domain randomization and system identification techniques to bridge the gap. Their experiments demonstrate improved policy transferability, highlighting Isaac Sim/Gym's role as a scalable and physically accurate training environment for real robotic deployment.

- ["Reinforcement learning for quadruped robot locomotion control" (2024)](https://openalex.org/W7112725633) — The HKU Scholars Hub (University of Hong Kong) — This paper explores reinforcement learning for quadruped robot locomotion control in complex terrain, combining model predictive control (MPC) with RL to improve robustness and adaptability. It mentions using Isaac Gym as the simulation platform for training the RL policy through environment interaction and reward maximization. The approach is validated on a physical quadruped robot, demonstrating the practical transfer of policies learned in simulation.

- ["Automated Hyperparameter Tuning in Reinforcement Learning for Quadrupedal Robot Locomotion" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4390266979) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2079-9292/13/1/116/pdf?version=1703674774) (Citations: 7) — Electronics — This paper proposes an automated method for tuning the scale of dominant reward functions in reinforcement learning for quadrupedal robot locomotion, aiming to improve gait stability and reduce manual reward shaping effort. The approach evaluates agents based on a custom gait score that reflects walking stability and is tested on two robots of different sizes. However, the paper does not mention or utilize Isaac Sim as the simulation environment or platform; instead, it appears to rely on other simulation tools without specifying them.

- ["MEP-SEG DATASET : SYNTHETIC IMAGES GENERATED FROM BUILDING INFORMATION MODELING (BIM)" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4392686425) — HAL (Le Centre pour la Communication Scientifique Directe) — The MEP-SEG dataset introduces 8,751 synthetic RGB images with corresponding semantic segmentation masks and labels for 13 building-related classes, generated by transferring three Autodesk Revit construction models into NVIDIA Isaac Sim. This work leverages Isaac Sim’s high-fidelity rendering and simulation capabilities to create a domain-specific dataset for training computer vision models in building information modeling (BIM) contexts. The dataset addresses the scarcity of labeled data in MEP (mechanical, electrical, plumbing) systems and supports tasks like automated facility inspection or digital twin development.

- ["Learning and Reusing Quadruped Robot Movement Skills from Biological Dogs for Higher-Level Tasks" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4390007869) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1424-8220/24/1/28/pdf?version=1703059553) (Citations: 3) — Sensors — This paper proposes a hierarchical reinforcement learning framework that enables quadruped robots to learn basic locomotion skills from biological dog motion data and then reuse these skills for higher-level tasks, bypassing the need for low-level kinematic control. Although the method leverages domain randomization for sim-to-real transfer, the abstract and available metadata do not mention Isaac Sim or any NVIDIA simulation platform as the training environment. The work focuses on algorithmic contributions rather than Isaac Sim-specific tools, datasets, or integrations.

- ["DARLEI: Deep Accelerated Reinforcement Learning with Evolutionary Intelligence" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2312.05171v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.05171v1) — DARLEI introduces a hybrid framework combining evolutionary algorithms with parallel reinforcement learning to evolve UNIMAL agent morphologies, using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) and tournament-based selection. Crucially, it builds directly on NVIDIA's Isaac Gym—part of the Isaac Sim ecosystem—to exploit GPU-accelerated physics simulation, achieving over 20x training speedup on a single workstation compared to prior CPU-cluster approaches. The work includes experiments on morphological diversity and inter-agent collisions within Isaac Gym, demonstrating its role as the core simulation platform for scalable embodied AI research.

- ["Multi Actor-Critic DDPG for Robot Action Space Decomposition: A Framework to Control Large 3D Deformation of Soft Linear Objects" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4389501449) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.04308) (Citations: 2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces MultiAC6, a multi Actor-Critic DDPG framework that decomposes robot action space to control large 3D deformations of deformable linear objects (DLOs). It uses two deep reinforcement learning agents to separately manage gripper orientation and position, enabling accurate manipulation without requiring analytical deformation models. Although the method achieves strong real-world performance with a 66% higher success rate than single-agent baselines and generalizes across DLO types, the abstract and available metadata do not mention Isaac Sim as the simulation environment or training platform.

- ["PolyFit: A Peg-in-hole Assembly Framework for Unseen Polygon Shapes via Sim-to-real Adaptation" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4389421688) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.02531) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — PolyFit introduces a supervised learning framework for 5-DoF peg-in-hole assembly using Force/Torque (F/T) sensor data to estimate and correct extrinsic pose misalignments. The method trains on a diverse simulated dataset of polygonal peg-hole pairs with corresponding F/T readings and employs a multi-point contact strategy to resolve pose ambiguities. Although the paper leverages simulation for training and proposes a sim-to-real adaptation technique, it does not explicitly mention or utilize Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, instead treating simulation generically.

- ["Robust Conformal Prediction for STL Runtime Verification under Distribution Shift" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2311.09482v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.09482v2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper proposes Robust Predictive Runtime Verification (RPRV) algorithms for Signal Temporal Logic (STL) tasks in stochastic cyber-physical systems, addressing distribution shifts between simulation and real-world deployment. The authors leverage robust conformal prediction with known bounds on f-divergence to provide statistical guarantees under distribution shift, using a trajectory prediction model for runtime behavior estimation. They validate their approach empirically on a Franka manipulator within the NVIDIA Isaac Sim environment, demonstrating the first statistically valid runtime verification method under such shifts.

- ["MetaGraspNetV2: All-in-One Dataset Enabling Fast and Reliable Robotic Bin Picking via Object Relationship Reasoning and Dexterous Grasping" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4388407473) [PDF](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/ielx7/8856/4358066/10309974.pdf) (Citations: 22) — IEEE Transactions on Automation Science and Engineering — MetaGraspNetV2 introduces a comprehensive bin picking dataset designed to enable holistic robotic perception and grasping by integrating multiple tasks—such as amodal panoptic segmentation, object relationship reasoning, 6-DoF pose estimation, and dual-gripper grasp detection—into a unified framework. The photo-realistic portion of the dataset is generated via physics-based metaverse synthesis, though the paper does not explicitly state that Isaac Sim was used as the simulation platform. Despite its relevance to robotic simulation and grasping, the work lacks direct mention or use of Isaac Sim, Isaac Gym, Isaac Lab, or Nvidia Newton.

- ["A ROS 2 and TwinCAT Based Digital Twin Framework for Mechatronics Systems" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4390485878) (Citations: 8) — This paper introduces a digital twin framework for mechatronics systems that integrates real-time control via TwinCAT 3, ROS 2 middleware, and NVIDIA Isaac Sim as the virtual simulation environment. The authors use Isaac Sim to create a high-fidelity virtual representation of a load-handling system, enabling synchronization between physical hardware and simulation for autonomous operation development. By leveraging Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated physics and rendering, the framework provides a scalable foundation for building digital twins across diverse mechatronic applications.

- ["Software Framework of Autonomous Mobile Robots on Isaac Sim and ROS" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4390481090) (Citations: 2) — This paper presents a software framework for autonomous mobile robots (AMRs) in military and factory settings, integrating Isaac Sim with the ROS framework to enable AI-driven navigation, sensor fusion, obstacle detection, and formation control. The authors demonstrate successful execution of predefined movement sequences using Isaac Sim as the simulation environment, showcasing path planning and position control capabilities. The work highlights Isaac Sim's role in developing and validating AMR behaviors before real-world deployment.

- ["RANS: Highly-Parallelised Simulator for Reinforcement Learning based Autonomous Navigating Spacecrafts" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2310.07393v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.07393v1) — The paper introduces RANS, an open-source simulator built on NVIDIA Isaac Gym to enable highly parallelized reinforcement learning for autonomous spacecraft navigation. By leveraging GPU-accelerated physics and policy training within Isaac Gym, RANS supports thousands of concurrent spacecraft simulations performing complex maneuvering tasks like position, attitude, and velocity control—critical for space operations such as docking and landing. This work directly utilizes Isaac Sim’s underlying framework (Isaac Gym) as its core simulation and training platform, making it a relevant contribution to the Isaac Sim ecosystem.

- ["Terrain-adaptive Central Pattern Generators with Reinforcement Learning for Hexapod Locomotion" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2310.07744v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.07744v1) — This paper proposes a terrain-adaptive locomotion control method for hexapod robots by integrating deep reinforcement learning (DRL) with central pattern generators (CPGs). The CPG generates synchronized gait signals, while DRL dynamically adjusts CPG parameters to improve adaptability on uneven terrains. Experiments are conducted in the Isaac Gym simulation environment, demonstrating enhanced terrain adaptability, faster convergence, and reduced reward design complexity. The work highlights Isaac Gym as a critical platform for training and validating legged locomotion policies in complex simulated environments.

- ["OmniDrones: An Efficient and Flexible Platform for Reinforcement Learning in Drone Control" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2309.12825v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.12825v1) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — OmniDrones introduces an efficient, open-source reinforcement learning platform specifically designed for drone control, built directly on NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim. It leverages Isaac Sim’s GPU-parallelized physics simulation to support diverse drone models, sensor modalities, control modes, and over 10 benchmark tasks—from basic hovering to complex over-actuated tracking. The platform includes standard RL baselines and preliminary results, offering a comprehensive toolkit to accelerate research in practical drone autonomy using Isaac Sim as its core simulation environment.

- ["NeuroMechFly v2, simulating embodied sensorimotor control in adult <i>Drosophila</i>" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4386819513) [PDF](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/biorxiv/early/2023/10/09/2023.09.18.556649.full.pdf) (Citations: 15) — NeuroMechFly v2 introduces a neuromechanical simulation framework for adult Drosophila that integrates visual and olfactory sensing, ascending motor feedback, and complex terrain navigation. While the study leverages advanced simulation capabilities, it does not use or focus on NVIDIA Isaac Sim as its platform; instead, it builds upon custom or alternative physics and neural modeling tools. The work enables biologically grounded sensorimotor control research and reinforcement learning applications but lacks direct integration with or contribution to Isaac Sim.

- ["OmniLRS: A Photorealistic Simulator for Lunar Robotics" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08997v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.08997v1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces OmniLRS, a photorealistic lunar robotics simulator built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim, designed to support algorithm development and evaluation for lunar exploration. It leverages Isaac Sim’s capabilities to offer procedural terrain generation, multi-robot simulation, ROS1/ROS2 integration, and a synthetic data pipeline for machine learning. The authors demonstrate sim-to-real transfer by training a YOLOv8 model on synthetic lunar rock data, achieving strong performance—within 5% of real-data training—and surpassing it by 14% in average precision after fine-tuning, highlighting the simulator’s realism and utility for perception tasks.

- ["Bridging Locomotion and Manipulation Using Reconfigurable Robotic Limbs via Reinforcement Learning" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385805393) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2313-7673/8/4/364/pdf?version=1691989509) (Citations: 13) — Biomimetics — This paper presents a unified reinforcement learning approach to bridge locomotion and manipulation by using reconfigurable, overconstrained robotic limbs that can function as both legs and fingers. The authors train a single policy—using either a multilayer perceptron or graph neural network—in simulation to perform both tasks, demonstrating skill transferability between domains. Although the work mentions Sim2Real transfer, it does not specify the use of Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, nor does it focus on Isaac Sim as a core component of its methodology or contributions.

- ["Towards Building AI-CPS with NVIDIA Isaac Sim: An Industrial Benchmark and Case Study for Robotics Manipulation" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2308.00055v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.00055v1) (Citations: 23) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper introduces a public benchmark for AI-enabled robotic manipulation using NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim as the core simulation platform. It includes eight representative manipulation tasks and multiple AI controllers, accompanied by extensive empirical evaluations to assess controller performance. The authors also develop the first falsification framework compatible with Isaac Sim, integrating traditional falsification techniques with modern physics-based simulation to enhance reliability analysis of AI-CPS systems.

- ["Parallel $Q$-Learning: Scaling Off-policy Reinforcement Learning under Massively Parallel Simulation" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2307.12983v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.12983v1) — This paper introduces Parallel Q-Learning (PQL), an off-policy reinforcement learning method specifically designed to leverage massively parallel GPU-based simulation environments like Isaac Gym. By concurrently parallelizing data collection, policy updates, and value learning on a single workstation, PQL achieves faster wall-clock training than on-policy methods such as PPO while preserving the sample efficiency of off-policy algorithms. The authors demonstrate successful scaling to tens of thousands of parallel environments within Isaac Gym, offering a practical approach for accelerating RL training in high-throughput simulated robotics tasks.

- ["Sampling-based Model Predictive Control Leveraging Parallelizable Physics Simulations" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2307.09105v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.09105v3) (Citations: 6) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces a sampling-based Model Predictive Path Integral (MPPI) controller that leverages the GPU-parallelizable Isaac Gym physics simulator as a differentiable, black-box dynamics model, eliminating the need for explicit robot or contact modeling. By using Isaac Sim's Isaac Gym environment to simulate thousands of trajectories in parallel, the method enables efficient control for complex, contact-rich tasks such as mobile navigation, non-prehensile manipulation, and whole-body control. This approach provides an accessible, general-purpose framework for high-dimensional robotic control without hand-crafted dynamics.

- ["Application of Reinforcement Learning to UR10 Positioning for Prioritized Multi-Step Inspection in NVIDIA Omniverse" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385830952) (Citations: 6) — This paper investigates the use of reinforcement learning (RL) to automate multi-step robot positioning for inspection tasks in dynamic, mass-customization manufacturing environments. The authors implement and compare state-of-the-art RL algorithms—DDPG, TD3, TRPO, and PPO—within the NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim environment to train a UR10 robotic arm. Their experiments show that TRPO achieves the highest accuracy in reaching sequential inspection waypoints. By leveraging Isaac Sim’s GPU-accelerated simulation capabilities, the work demonstrates a practical approach to adaptive robotic inspection systems under uncertainty.

- ["SynTable: A Synthetic Data Generation Pipeline for Unseen Object Amodal Instance Segmentation of Cluttered Tabletop Scenes" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2307.07333v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.07333v3) (Citations: 4) — arXiv (Cornell University) — SynTable introduces a synthetic data generation pipeline built using NVIDIA's Isaac Sim Replicator Composer to create high-quality, photorealistic datasets for amodal instance segmentation in cluttered tabletop scenes. The tool automatically generates rich annotations—including modal/amodal masks, depth maps, occlusion masks, and bounding boxes—without manual labeling, and demonstrates strong Sim-to-Real transfer by training UOAIS-Net with its synthetic data. By leveraging Isaac Sim as the core rendering and scene-composition platform, SynTable provides an open-source, flexible solution for advancing synthetic data research in robotics and computer vision.

- ["A Planning Framework for Robotic Insertion Tasks via Hydroelastic Contact Model" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4384525704) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2075-1702/11/7/741/pdf?version=1689652461) (Citations: 6) — Machines — This paper proposes a motion planning framework for robotic insertion tasks that combines Dynamic Movement Primitives (DMPs) and Black-Box Optimization (BBO) with a hydroelastic contact model to improve simulation accuracy while maintaining computational efficiency. The authors validate their approach using a Peg-in-Hole benchmark task, comparing simulated contact behavior against real-world results. However, the work does not mention or utilize Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, instead relying on a custom or unspecified simulator implementing the hydroelastic contact model.
