- ["Pegasus Simulator: An Isaac Sim Framework for Multiple Aerial Vehicles Simulation" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2307.05263v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.05263v2) (Citations: 23) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces Pegasus Simulator, a modular NVIDIA Isaac Sim extension designed for real-time, photo-realistic simulation of multiple multirotor aerial vehicles. It provides seamless integration with PX4-Autopilot and ROS2 through a user-friendly interface and demonstrates its capabilities with aggressive flight maneuvers by two drones using a custom nonlinear controller. The framework significantly lowers the barrier for developing and testing multi-vehicle aerial robotics algorithms within Isaac Sim.

- ["IndustReal: Transferring Contact-Rich Assembly Tasks from Simulation to Reality" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385430467) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2023.xix.039) (Citations: 36) — The paper introduces IndustReal, a framework that enables successful transfer of contact-rich robotic assembly policies from simulation to reality using reinforcement learning. While the authors solve tasks from the Factory benchmark—which is part of NVIDIA's Isaac Sim ecosystem—they do not explicitly state that they use Isaac Sim as their simulation platform; instead, they reference Factory [48] as the source of tasks. The work focuses on algorithmic contributions like simulation-aware policy updates and a policy-level action integrator, but lacks direct evidence of Isaac Sim usage or integration.

- ["Rotating without Seeing: Towards In-hand Dexterity through Touch" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385430564) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2023.xix.036) (Citations: 69) — This paper presents a touch-only approach to in-hand dexterous manipulation, specifically object rotation, using a robot hand equipped with dense binary force sensors covering the palm and fingers. The authors train a reinforcement learning policy entirely in simulation using only tactile feedback and successfully deploy it on a real robot without fine-tuning, demonstrating generalization to novel objects. Although the work leverages simulation for training, it does not explicitly mention or use Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, instead focusing on sensor design and policy learning.

- ["AnyTeleop: A General Vision-Based Dexterous Robot Arm-Hand Teleoperation System" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385430618) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2023.xix.015) (Citations: 72) — AnyTeleop introduces a general vision-based teleoperation system for dexterous robot arm-hand control, demonstrating superior performance over hardware-specific and simulator-specific baselines. The work leverages NVIDIA Isaac Sim for simulated teleoperation experiments, showing improved imitation learning outcomes compared to prior methods tailored to that simulator. This highlights Isaac Sim's utility as a testbed for developing and evaluating generalizable teleoperation frameworks.

- ["Sampling-based Exploration for Reinforcement Learning of Dexterous Manipulation" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385403828) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2023.xix.020) (Citations: 25) — This paper proposes a sampling-based exploration method using non-holonomic Rapidly-Exploring Random Trees to improve reinforcement learning for dexterous manipulation, particularly by generating effective reset distributions that enable policy training in complex, high-dimensional state spaces. However, the work does not mention or utilize Isaac Sim as the simulation environment or platform; experiments appear to be conducted in other simulators or custom setups. As such, it lacks direct relevance to the Isaac Sim-focused Awesome list.

- ["Integrated Object Deformation and Contact Patch Estimation from Visuo-Tactile Feedback" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4385416139) [PDF](https://doi.org/10.15607/rss.2023.xix.080) (Citations: 7) — The paper introduces Neural Deforming Contact Field (NDCF), an implicit representation that jointly models object deformation and contact patches using visuo-tactile feedback. The authors train their model on simulated data and demonstrate zero-shot transfer to real-world scenarios, outperforming point-cloud-based baselines. However, the paper does not mention or use Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, instead relying on unspecified simulation tools for data generation.

- ["LAGOON: Language-Guided Motion Control" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4381557820) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.10518) (Citations: 3) — arXiv (Cornell University) — LAGOON introduces a multi-phase reinforcement learning approach to enable robots to execute diverse physical behaviors from natural language commands. The method first uses a pretrained model to generate human motion from text, then trains a control policy in simulation to imitate this motion, and finally applies domain randomization to transfer the policy to a real quadrupedal robot. Although the paper uses simulation for training, it does not explicitly mention or rely on Isaac Sim as the simulation platform, instead using generic simulation environments.

- ["DeXtreme: Transfer of Agile In-hand Manipulation from Simulation to Reality" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4383108265) (Citations: 83) — DeXtreme demonstrates robust sim-to-real transfer of dexterous in-hand manipulation using deep reinforcement learning, specifically training policies in Isaac Gym—a GPU-accelerated simulation environment within the NVIDIA Isaac Sim ecosystem. The authors develop a vision-based policy and a real-time object pose estimator that enable an Allegro Hand to reorient objects with performance competitive against motion-capture-based systems. By leveraging domain randomization and extensive simulation training in Isaac Gym, the work achieves high real-world success rates and shows that affordable hardware can be used for complex manipulation tasks.

- ["Aerial Gym -- Isaac Gym Simulator for Aerial Robots" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16510v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.16510v1) — The paper introduces Aerial Gym, a highly parallelized simulator built on Isaac Gym for training aerial robots using reinforcement learning. It enables simulation of millions of multirotor vehicles with nonlinear geometric controllers on SE(3) for precise attitude, velocity, and position tracking, while supporting customizable obstacle randomization and simulated RGB-D, segmentation, and optical flow sensing. By providing compatible RL training scripts and open-sourcing the platform, the work addresses a critical gap in scalable, realistic aerial robot simulation within the Isaac Sim ecosystem.

- ["DexPBT: Scaling up Dexterous Manipulation for Hand-Arm Systems with Population Based Training" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2305.12127v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.12127v1) (Citations: 20) — DexPBT introduces a decentralized Population-Based Training (PBT) algorithm to enhance deep reinforcement learning for dexterous manipulation tasks using simulated hand-arm systems. The work leverages Isaac Gym—a GPU-accelerated physics simulator within the Isaac Sim ecosystem—to train policies for complex tasks like regrasping, grasp-and-throw, and object reorientation. By enabling massive parallelization and improved exploration, DexPBT significantly outperforms standard end-to-end learning approaches, yielding robust and transferable manipulation policies with practical implications for real-world robotic systems.

- ["A Fast 6DOF Visual Selective Grasping System Using Point Clouds" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4381679720) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/2075-1702/11/5/540/pdf?version=1683769569) (Citations: 2) — Machines — This paper presents a fast 6DOF visual selective grasping system that uses point clouds and a novel deep learning architecture called Point Encoder Convolution (PEC) for object classification. The system was trained on a dataset generated in a realistic simulator and achieves a 94% success rate with a runtime of approximately 0.004 seconds. However, the abstract does not explicitly mention Isaac Sim or any NVIDIA simulation platform as the source of the simulated data.

- ["Deep Learning for Ultrasound Speed-of-Sound Reconstruction: Impacts of Training Data Diversity on Stability and Robustness" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4376878291) (Citations: 12) — The Journal of Machine Learning for Biomedical Imaging — This paper investigates how training data diversity affects the stability and robustness of deep learning models for ultrasound speed-of-sound reconstruction, proposing a novel simulation setup based on Tomosynthesis images combined with simplified geometric models. However, the work does not mention or utilize Isaac Sim, Isaac Lab, Isaac Gym, or any NVIDIA simulation platform; it focuses solely on medical ultrasound simulation and reconstruction using custom or unspecified simulation tools.

- ["Enhancing Efficiency of Quadrupedal Locomotion over Challenging Terrains with Extensible Feet" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2305.01998v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.01998v1) — This paper introduces a novel deep reinforcement learning locomotion policy for a quadrupedal robot with extensible feet—achieved via added prismatic joints—and trains it exclusively in the NVIDIA Isaac Gym simulation environment. The study evaluates how these extra degrees of freedom improve terrain adaptability and energy efficiency, using Cost of Transport (CoT) as the key metric across challenging simulated terrains. Results show that the modified design significantly outperforms conventional quadrupeds in both feasibility and efficiency, demonstrating Isaac Gym’s utility for rapid, physics-accurate legged locomotion research.

- ["Digital Twin of a Multi-Arm Robot Platform based on Isaac Sim for Synthetic Data Generation" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4393538722) — Zenodo (CERN European Organization for Nuclear Research) — This paper presents a digital twin of a multi-arm robot platform developed in Isaac Sim specifically for generating synthetic data to support pretraining in robotics. The authors leverage Isaac Sim's high-fidelity, GPU-accelerated simulation capabilities to create realistic sensor and motion data from complex multi-arm interactions. The resulting dataset is publicly released to facilitate research in sim-to-real transfer and robotic learning.

- ["DribbleBot: Dynamic Legged Manipulation in the Wild" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4362599025) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.01159) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — DribbleBot presents a legged robotic system capable of dribbling a soccer ball in real-world, unstructured environments by training reinforcement learning policies in simulation and transferring them to hardware. While the paper leverages simulation-to-reality transfer, it does not explicitly mention or focus on Isaac Sim as the simulation platform—instead using a custom or unspecified simulator. The work highlights challenges in terrain variability and onboard visual perception but lacks direct contribution to or evaluation within Isaac Sim.

- ["Multi-Task Reinforcement Learning in Continuous Control with Successor Feature-Based Concurrent Composition" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13935v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.13935v2) — This paper introduces a multi-task reinforcement learning approach that unifies successor feature-based general policy improvement (SF-GPI) and value composition to enable zero-shot transfer in continuous control tasks. The authors leverage the compositional properties of successor features to combine pre-trained primitive policies without additional training. To validate their method, they implement custom benchmark environments—Pointmass and Pointer—using Isaac Gym, taking advantage of its GPU-accelerated parallel simulation capabilities for efficient large-scale experimentation. The approach matches SAC-level performance on individual tasks while enabling successful generalization to unseen tasks, offering a sample-efficient framework for robotic control.

- ["GRADE: Generating Realistic And Dynamic Environments for Robotics Research with Isaac Sim" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04466v3) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.04466v3) (Citations: 2) — arXiv (Cornell University) — The paper introduces GRADE, a framework built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim to generate realistic and dynamic synthetic environments for robotics research. It leverages Isaac Sim’s rendering capabilities and low-level APIs to enable precise control over dynamic scenes, ground-truth data collection, and reproducible experiments with physical fidelity. The authors demonstrate its utility by creating a richly annotated drone-based dataset in dynamic settings, used to train human detection and segmentation models, thereby helping bridge the sim-to-real gap in visual perception tasks.

- ["Cutaneous Feedback Interface for Teleoperated In-Hand Manipulation" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2303.03250v1) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.03250v1) — This paper proposes a cutaneous feedback interface using five-bar link mechanisms to provide tactile cues on the index finger and thumb, enhancing human-in-the-loop teleoperation for in-hand manipulation tasks. The system integrates with a commercial haptic device to convey grasping force, shear force, friction, and object pose, and its effectiveness is evaluated through passive pivoting tasks conducted in the Isaac Sim simulator. The use of Isaac Sim as the experimental platform highlights its utility for testing human-robot interaction systems in realistic, physics-rich environments.

- ["A Multi-Layered 3D NDT Scan-Matching Method for Robust Localization in Logistics Warehouse Environments" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4322743802) [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1424-8220/23/5/2671/pdf?version=1677594334) (Citations: 4) — Sensors — This paper proposes a multi-layered 3D Normal Distribution Transform (NDT) scan-matching method to enhance localization robustness in dynamic warehouse environments by partitioning point clouds into height-based layers and selecting those with lowest uncertainty for pose estimation. The authors validate their approach using NVIDIA Omniverse Isaac Sim, leveraging its simulation capabilities to model cluttered logistics scenarios and evaluate performance under environmental changes. The method offers a practical solution for mobile robot navigation in occlusion-prone settings by dynamically adapting to reliable spatial features.

- ["Trend of Robot Simulators for the Development of Intelligent Robots: A Review" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4324336845) (Citations: 1) — Journal of Korean institute of intelligent systems — This review paper analyzes five physics-based robot simulators—Gazebo, Isaac Sim, Mujoco, CoppeliaSim, and Webots—focusing on their use in intelligent robot development, particularly for AI training and virtual testing. It highlights Isaac Sim as a GPU-accelerated platform that enables both physics simulation and deep learning on the GPU, mitigating CPU-GPU bottlenecks in deep reinforcement learning. The paper examines 11 studies using Isaac Sim to demonstrate its advantages in simulation speed and deformable object modeling.

- ["Reinforcement Learning Based Pushing and Grasping Objects from Ungraspable Poses" (2023)](https://openalex.org/W4322716936) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.13328) (Citations: 1) — arXiv (Cornell University) — This paper presents a model-free deep reinforcement learning approach that combines pushing and grasping to manipulate objects from ungraspable poses, such as flat items lying horizontally. The method uses a shared Proximal Policy Optimization network with a pre-trained Variational Autoencoder for visual feature extraction and demonstrates improved data efficiency and generalization to unseen objects. Although the work mentions sim-to-real transfer using CycleGAN, it does not explicitly use or evaluate within Isaac Sim, nor is Isaac Sim central to its methodology or experiments.

- ["Orbit: A Unified Simulation Framework for Interactive Robot Learning Environments" (2023)](http://arxiv.org/abs/2301.04195v2) [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2301.04195v2) (Citations: 181) — IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters — Orbit is a unified, modular framework for robot learning built on NVIDIA Isaac Sim, enabling rapid creation of high-fidelity, photo-realistic robotic environments with support for rigid and deformable body physics. It provides 16 robotic platforms, over 20 benchmark tasks (e.g., cabinet opening, room reorganization), multiple sensor modalities, and GPU-accelerated parallelization for efficient reinforcement learning and dataset collection. By tightly integrating with Isaac Sim as its core simulation backbone, Orbit significantly lowers the barrier to developing and benchmarking interactive robot learning systems.
